\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{1}{{\sigma_{\text{err}}}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{R_{1}R_{2}}+\left(\frac{1}{R_{1}}+\frac{1}{R_{2}}\right)\cdot k^{5}\cdot n^{-2+(100\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)\right)

(97)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100}\text{.}

The last inequality holds by our choice of
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
as follows where
c
is a large enough constant that cancels the constant hidden in
O\left(\cdot\right)
in (
97
).

\min(R_{1},R_{2})\geq\frac{c\cdot k^{5}\cdot n^{-2+(100\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}^{2}}

and

R_{1}R_{2}\geq\frac{c\cdot k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}^{2}}

∎

Lemma 27
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular and
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph. Let
\sigma_{\text{err}}>0
and let
s>0
,
R>0
,
t>0
be integers. Let
I_{S}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}\}
be a multiset of
s
indices chosen from

\{1,\dots,n\}
. Let
S
be the
n\times s
matrix whose
j
-th column equals
\mathds{1}_{i_{j}}
. Let
c>1
be a large enough constant. Let
R\geq\max\left\{\frac{c\cdot k^{5}\cdot n^{-2+100\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}^{2}},\frac{c\cdot k\cdot n^{-1/2+20\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}}\right\}

Let
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times s}
be the output of Algorithm
EstimateCollisionProbabilities(
G,I_{S},R,t
)
(Algorithm
2
). Let
M
be the random walk transition matrix of
G
.
then with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
we have

\|\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}\leq s\cdot\sigma_{\text{err}}\text{.}

Proof.

Note that as per line (
2
) and (
3
) of Algorithm
2
we first construct matrices
\widehat{P}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s}
and
\widehat{Q}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s}
using Algorithm
3
. as per line (
3
) of Algorithm
3
matrix
\widehat{P}_{i}
(or
\widehat{Q}_{i}
) has
s
columns each corresponds to a vertex
x\in S
. The column corresponding to vertex
x
is
\widehat{m}_{x}
. as per line
2
of Algorithm
3
,
\widehat{m}_{x}
is defined as the empirical probability distribution of running
R
random walks of length
t
starting from vertex
x
. Thus for any
x,y\in S
we have the entry corresponding to the
x^{\text{th}}
row and
y^{\text{th}}
column of
\widehat{Q}_{i}^{T}\widehat{P}_{i}
(or
\widehat{P}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Q}_{i}
) is
\langle\widehat{m}_{x},\widehat{m}_{y}\rangle
. Since

R\geq\max\left\{\frac{c\cdot k^{5}\cdot n^{-2+100\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}^{2}},\frac{c\cdot k\cdot n^{-1/2+20\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}}\right\}

then by Lemma
26
with probability at least
0.99
we have

|\widehat{m}_{x}^{T}\widehat{m}_{y}-(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})|\leq\sigma_{\text{err}}\text{.}

Note that as per line
4
of Algorithm
2
we define

\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\frac{1}{2}\left(\widehat{P}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Q}_{i}+\widehat{Q}_{i}^{T}\widehat{P}_{i}\right)
. Thus for any
x,y\in I_{S}
we have the entry corresponding to the
x^{\text{th}}
row and
y^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
(i.e.,
\mathcal{G}_{i}(x,y)
) with probability
0.99
satisfies the following:

|\mathcal{G}_{i}(x,y)-(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})|\leq\sigma_{\text{err}}\text{.}

Note that as Line
5
of Algorithm
2
we define
\mathcal{G}
as a matrix obtained by taking the entrywises median of
\mathcal{G}_{i}
’s over
O(\log n)
runs. Thus with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
we have for all
x,y\in I_{S}

|\mathcal{G}(x,y)-(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})|\leq\sigma_{\text{err}}\text{.}

which implies

\|\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)^{T}\|_{F}\leq s\cdot{\sigma_{\text{err}}}\text{.}

Since the Frobenius norm of a matrix bounds its maximum eigenvalue from above we get

\|\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)^{T}\|_{2}\leq s\cdot{\sigma_{\text{err}}}\text{.}

∎

Recall that for a symmetric matrix
A
, we write
\nu_{i}(A)
(resp.
\nu_{\max}(A),\nu_{\min}(A))
to denote the
i^{\text{th}}
largest (resp. maximum, minimum) eigenvalue of
A
.

Lemma 28
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular and
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph. Let
t\geq\frac{20\log n}{\varphi^{2}}
. Let
c>1
be a large enough constant and
s\geq c\cdot n^{240\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log n\cdot k^{4}
. Let
I_{S}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}\}
be a multiset of
s
indices chosen independently and uniformly at random from

\{1,\dots,n\}
. Let
S
be the
n\times s
matrix whose
j
-th column equals
\mathds{1}_{i_{j}}
. Let
M
be the random walk transition matrix of
G
.
If
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{10^{5}}
then with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
we have

1.

\nu_{k}\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)(M^{t}S)^{T}\right)\geq\frac{n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{2}

2.

\nu_{k+1}\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)(M^{t}S)^{T}\right)\leq n^{-9}\text{.}

Proof.

Let
(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
be an orthonormal basis of eigenvectors of
L
with corresponding eigenvalues
0\leq\lambda_{1}\leq\ldots\leq\lambda_{n}
.
Observe that each
u_{i}
is also an eigenvector of
M
, with eigenvalue
1-\frac{\lambda_{i}}{2}
.
Note that
G
is
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable, therefore by Lemma
3
we have
\lambda_{k}\leq 2\epsilon
and
\lambda_{k+1}\geq\frac{\varphi^{2}}{2}
. We have

\nu_{k+1}(M^{2t})=\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{2}\right)^{2t}\leq n^{-10}\text{, and}

(98)

\nu_{k}(M^{2t})=\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{k}}{2}\right)^{2t}\geq n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}

(99)

Proof of item (
1
):

Let
A=(M^{t})\left(M^{t}\right)^{T}
, and
\widetilde{A}=\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)\left(M^{t}S\right)^{T}
. By Lemma
22
we have

B=\|(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}\|_{2}\leq\|M^{t}\mathds{x}\mathds{1}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq O\left(k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)\text{.}

Let
\xi=n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}/2
.
Therefore for large enough constant
c
and by choice of
s=c\cdot k^{4}n^{240\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\log n
we have
s\geq\frac{40n^{2}B^{2}\log n}{(\xi)^{2}}
. Thus Lemma
21

yields that with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{n^{100}}
we have

\|A-\widetilde{A}\|_{2}\leq\frac{n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{2}\text{.}

(100)

Hence, by Weyl’s Inequality (see Lemma
17
) we have

\nu_{k}(\widetilde{A})\geq\nu_{k}(A)+\nu_{\min}(\widetilde{A}-A)=\nu_{k}(A)-\nu_{\max}(A-\widetilde{A})=\nu_{k}(A)-\|A-\widetilde{A}\|_{2}

By (
99
) we have
\nu_{k}(A)=\nu_{k}(M^{2t})\geq n^{-10\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}
and so

\nu_{k}(\widetilde{A})\geq\nu_{k}(A)-\|\widetilde{A}-A\|_{2}\geq n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}-\frac{n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{2}\geq\frac{n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{2}\text{.}

Proof of item (
2
):
By Lemma
8
we have

\displaystyle\nu_{k+1}(\widetilde{A})=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}((M^{t}S)(M^{t}S)^{T})=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}((M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S))=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}(S^{T}{M}^{2t}S).

Recall that
1-\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\geq\cdots\geq 1-\frac{\lambda_{n}}{2}
are the eigenvalues of
M
, and
\Sigma
is the diagonal matrix of these eigenvalues in descending order, and
U
is the matrix whose columns are orthonormal eigenvectors of
M
arranged in descending order of their eigenvalues. We have
M^{2t}=U\Sigma^{2t}U^{T}
. Recall that
\Sigma_{[k]}
is
k\times k
diagonal matrix with entries
1-\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}\geq\cdots\geq 1-\frac{\lambda_{k}}{2}
, and
\Sigma_{-[k]}
is a
(n-k)\times(n-k)
diagonal matrix with entries
1-\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{2}\geq\cdots\geq 1-\frac{\lambda_{n}}{2}
. We can write
U\Sigma^{2t}U=U_{[k]}\Sigma_{[k]}^{2t}U_{[k]}^{T}+U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}
, thus we get

\displaystyle\nu_{k+1}(\widetilde{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}\left(S^{T}M^{2t}S\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}\left(S^{T}(U\Sigma^{2t}U^{T})S\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}\left(S^{T}\left(U_{[k]}\Sigma_{[k]}^{2t}U_{[k]}^{T}+U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}\right)S\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{k+1}\left(S^{T}U_{[k]}\Sigma_{[k]}^{2t}U_{[k]}^{T}S\right)+\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{\max}\left(S^{T}U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}S\right)

By Weyl’s inequality (Lemma
17
)

Here
\nu_{k+1}(S^{T}U_{[k]}\Sigma_{[k]}^{2t}U_{[k]}^{T}S)=0
, because the rank of
\Sigma_{[k]}^{2t}
is
k
. We then need to bound
\nu_{\max}(S^{T}U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}S)
. We have,

\displaystyle\nu_{\max}\left(S^{T}U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}S\right)=\nu_{\max}\left(U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}SS^{\top}\right)

By Lemma
8

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{\max}\left(U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}\right)\cdot\nu_{\max}\left(SS^{\top}\right)

By submultiplicativity of norm

\displaystyle=\nu_{\max}\left(\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}U_{-[k]}\right)\cdot\nu_{\max}\left(SS^{\top}\right)

By Lemma
8

\displaystyle=\nu_{\max}\left(\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}\right)\cdot\nu_{\max}\left(SS^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle{\text{Since }U^{T}_{-[k]}U_{-[k]}=I}

Next, observe that
SS^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a diagonal matrix whose
(a,a)^{\text{\tiny{th}}}
entry is the multiplicity of vertex
a
is sampled in
S
. Thus,
\nu_{\max}(SS^{\top})
is the maximum multiplicity over all vertices, which is at most
s
. Also note that
\nu_{\max}(\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t})=\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{2}\right)^{2t}
. Thus by (
98
) we get,

\nu_{k+1}(\widetilde{A})\leq\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{\max}\left(S^{T}U_{-[k]}\Sigma_{-[k]}^{2t}U^{T}_{-[k]}S\right)\leq\frac{n}{s}\cdot s\cdot\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{k+1}}{2}\right)^{2t}\leq n\cdot n^{-10}=n^{-9}\text{.}

∎

Now we are ready to prove the main result of this section (Lemma
24
).
See
24

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{A}=\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}\left(M^{t}S\right)=\widetilde{W}\widetilde{\Sigma^{2}}\widetilde{W}^{T}
and
\widehat{A}=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\mathcal{G}
. Thus we have

\widetilde{A}^{2}=\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}\left(M^{t}S\right)\right)^{2}=\widetilde{W}\widetilde{\Sigma^{4}}\widetilde{W}^{T}

and

\widehat{A}^{2}=\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot\mathcal{G}\right)^{2}=\widehat{W}\widehat{\Sigma^{2}}\widehat{W}^{T}\text{.}

Recall that for a symmetric matrix
A
, we write
\nu_{i}(A)
to denote the
i^{\text{th}}
largest eigenvalue of
A
. We want to apply Lemma
18
to get

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}-\widehat{W}_{[k]}\widehat{\Sigma}_{[k]}^{-2}\widehat{W}_{[k]}^{T}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\cdot\|\widetilde{A}^{2}-\widehat{A}^{2}\|_{2}+4\cdot\nu_{k+1}(\widetilde{A}^{2})}{\nu_{k}(\widetilde{A}^{2})^{2}}

Hence, we first need to verify the prerequisites of Lemma
18
. Let
c_{3}>1
be a large enough constant that we will define soon, and let
\sigma_{\text{err}}=\frac{\xi\cdot n^{(-1-360\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{c_{3}\cdot k^{2}}
. Let
c
be a constant from Lemma
27
. By the assumption of the lemma for large enough constant
c_{2}>1
we have

R\geq\frac{c_{2}\cdot k^{9}\cdot n^{1/2+820\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\xi^{2}}\geq\max\left\{\frac{c\cdot k^{5}\cdot n^{-2+100\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}^{2}},\frac{c\cdot k\cdot n^{-1/2+20\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\sigma_{\text{err}}}\right\}\text{.}

Thus we can apply Lemma
27
. Hence, with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
we have

\|\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}\leq s\cdot\sigma_{\text{err}}\text{.}

(101)

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}^{2}-\left((M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\right)^{2}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{G}\left(\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\right)+\left(\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\right)(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{G}-(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}\left(\|\mathcal{G}\|_{2}+\|(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq s\cdot\sigma_{\text{err}}\left((s\cdot\sigma_{\text{err}}+\|(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2})+\|(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=(s\cdot\sigma_{\text{err}})^{2}+2\cdot s\cdot\sigma_{\text{err}}\|(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}

(102)

Note that

\displaystyle\|(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{x,y\in S}\left((M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{x,y\in S}\|M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\|M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y}\|_{2}^{2}}

By Cauchy Schwarz

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\sqrt{s^{2}\cdot\left(k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)^{2}}\right)

By Lemma
22

\displaystyle=O\left(s\cdot k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)

.

(103)

Puuting (
103
) and (
102
) and by choice of
\sigma_{\text{err}}=\frac{\xi\cdot n^{(-1-360\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{c_{3}\cdot k^{2}}
we get

\|\widetilde{A}^{2}-\widehat{A}^{2}\|_{2}=\left(\frac{n}{s}\right)^{2}\|\mathcal{G}^{2}-\left((M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\right)^{2}\|_{2}\leq O\left(\frac{\xi^{2}\cdot n^{-720\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{(c_{3})^{2}\cdot k^{4}}+\frac{\xi\cdot n^{-320\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{c_{3}}\right)=O\left(\frac{\xi\cdot n^{-320\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{c_{3}}\right)

(104)

By Lemma
8
for any
i\in[s]
we have

\nu_{i}(\widetilde{A})=\nu_{i}\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)\left(M^{t}S\right)^{T}\right)=\nu_{i}\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}\left(M^{t}S\right)\right)

Let
c_{1}
be the constant from Lemma
28
. Since
s\geq c_{1}\cdot n^{240\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log n\cdot k^{4}
therefore by Lemma
28
with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
we have

\nu_{k}{\left(\widetilde{A}^{2}\right)}=\nu_{k}{\left(\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}\left(M^{t}S\right)\right)^{2}\right)}\geq\left(\frac{n^{-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{2}\right)^{2}\geq\frac{n^{-160\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{4}

(105)

and

\nu_{k+1}{\left(\widetilde{A}^{2}\right)}=\nu_{k+1}{\left(\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}\left(M^{t}S\right)\right)^{2}\right)}\leq n^{-18}

(106)

By the bound on the
\nu_{k}{(\widetilde{A}^{2})}
and the inequality on
\|\widetilde{A}^{2}-\widehat{A}^{2}\|_{2}
, we know that
\nu_{k}{(\widehat{A}^{2})}
is non-zero and so
\widehat{\Sigma}_{[k]}^{-2}
exist.
Recall that
\widetilde{A}=\widetilde{W}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}\widetilde{W}^{T}\text{.}
Observing that
\widetilde{A}
is positive semi-definite,
\nu_{k+1}(\widetilde{A}^{2})<\nu_{k}(\widetilde{A}^{2})/4
,
and
\|\widetilde{A}^{2}-\widehat{A}^{2}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{100}\cdot\nu_{k}(\widetilde{A}^{2})
we can apply Lemma
18
and we get

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}-\widehat{W}_{[k]}\widehat{\Sigma}_{[k]}^{-2}\widehat{W}_{[k]}^{T}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\cdot\|\widetilde{A}^{2}-\widehat{A}^{2}\|_{2}+4\cdot\nu_{k+1}(\widetilde{A}^{2})}{\nu_{k}(\widetilde{A}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{O\left(\frac{\xi\cdot n^{(-320\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{c_{3}}\right)+4\cdot n^{-18}}{\frac{1}{16}\cdot n^{(-320\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}

By (
104
) and (
105
)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\xi}{c_{3}}\right)+64\cdot n^{-17}

\displaystyle\leq\xi

The last inequality holds since
\xi\geq n^{-8}
and by setting
c_{3}
to a large enough constant to cancel the constant hidden in
O\left(\frac{\xi}{{c_{3}}}\right)
.
∎

5.5
Proof of Theorem
2

See
2

To prove Theorem
2
we need to combine Lemma

from Section
5.3
with the following lemma.

Lemma 29
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular and
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph. Let
0<\delta<1/2
, and
1/n^{6}<\xi<1
.
Let
\mathcal{D}
denote the data structure constructed by Algorithm
InitializeOracle(
G,\delta,\xi
)
(Algorithm
4
).
Let
x,y\in V
. Let
\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\in\mathbb{R}
denote the value returned by

\textsc{SpectralDotProductOracle}(G,x,y,\delta,\xi,\mathcal{D})
(Algorithm
5
).
Let
t\geq\frac{20\log n}{\varphi^{2}}
. Let
c>1
be a large enough constant and let
s\geq c\cdot n^{240\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log n\cdot k^{4}
. Let
I_{S}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}\}
be a multiset of
s
indices chosen independently and uniformly at random from

\{1,\dots,n\}
. Let
S
be the
n\times s
matrix whose
j
-th column equals
\mathds{1}_{i_{j}}
. Let
M
be the random walk transition matrix of
G
. Let
\sqrt{\frac{n}{s}}\cdot M^{t}S=\widetilde{U}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{W}^{T}
be an SVD of
\sqrt{\frac{n}{s}}\cdot M^{t}S
where
\widetilde{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\widetilde{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\widetilde{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
. If
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{10^{5}}
, and Algorithm
4
succeeds, then with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
matrix
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{[k]}^{-4}
exists and we have

\left|\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}S)\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})\right|<\frac{\xi}{n}\text{.}

Proof.

Note that as per line
7
of Algorithm
5

\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
is defined as

\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}=\alpha_{x}^{T}\Psi\alpha_{y}\text{.}

where as per line
3
of Algorithm
4
we define matrix

as

\Psi=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\widehat{W}_{[k]}\widehat{\Sigma}_{[k]}^{-2}\widehat{W}_{[k]}^{T}\text{,}

and
\alpha_{x},\alpha_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
are vectors obtained by taking entrywise median over all
(\widehat{Q}_{i})^{T}(\widehat{m}^{i}_{x})
and
(\widehat{Q}_{i})^{T}(\widehat{m}^{i}_{y})
. (See line
5
and
6
of Algorithm
5
).
For any vertex
a\in V
recall that
m_{a}
denote
m_{a}=M^{t}\mathds{1}_{a}
. We then define

\mathbf{a}_{x}=m_{x}^{T}(M^{t}S),\quad A=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]},\quad\mathbf{a}_{y}=(M^{t}S)^{T}m_{y}\text{, and}

\mathbf{e}_{x}=\alpha_{x}^{T}-\mathbf{a}_{x},\quad E=\Psi-A,\quad\mathbf{e}_{y}=\alpha_{y}-\mathbf{a}_{y}

Thus by triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\alpha_{x}^{T}\Psi\alpha_{y}-m_{x}^{T}(M^{t}S)\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)(M^{t}S)^{T}m_{y}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\left(\mathbf{a}_{x}+\mathbf{e}_{x}\right)\left(A+E\right)\left(\mathbf{a}_{y}+\mathbf{e}_{y}\right)-\mathbf{a}_{x}A\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}

Therefore we need to bound
\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}
,
\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}
,
\|E\|_{2}
,
\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}
,
\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}
and
\|A\|_{2}
.
Let
c^{\prime}>1
be a constant we will define soon, and let
\xi^{\prime}=\frac{\xi}{c^{\prime}\cdot k^{4}\cdot n^{80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}
. Let
c_{1}
be a constant in front of
s
and let
c_{2}
be a constant in front of
R
in Lemma
24
. Thus for large enough
c
we have

s\geq c_{1}\cdot n^{240\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log n\cdot k^{4}
and
R_{\text{init}}=\varTheta{(n^{1-\delta+980\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot k^{17}/{\xi}^{2})}\geq\frac{c_{2}\cdot k^{9}\cdot n^{1/2+820\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{\xi^{\prime 2}}
as per line
2
of Algorithm
4
, hence, by Lemma
24
applied with
\xi^{\prime}
we have with probability at least
1-n^{-100}
,
\widehat{W}_{[k]}^{T}-
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}
exist and we have

\|E\|_{2}=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\left|\left|\widehat{W}_{[k]}\widehat{\Sigma}_{[k]}^{-2}\widehat{W}_{[k]}^{T}-\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{n}{s}\cdot\xi^{\prime}=\frac{\xi\cdot n}{c^{\prime}\cdot k^{4}\cdot n^{80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot s}\text{.}

(107)

Recall that for a symmetric matrix
A
, we write
\nu_{i}(A)
(resp.
\nu_{\max}(A),\nu_{\min}(A))
to denote the
i^{\text{th}}
largest (resp. maximum, minimum) eigenvalue of
A
. We have

\|A\|_{2}=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\|\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\|_{2}=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\nu_{\max}\left(\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)=\frac{n}{s}\cdot\frac{1}{\nu_{k}\left(\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)}

Note that
\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)=\widetilde{W}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}\widetilde{W}^{T}
. Thus by Lemma
28
item (
1
) we have

\nu_{k}\left(\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)=\nu_{k}\left(\widetilde{W}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{4}\widetilde{W}^{T}\right)=\nu_{k}\left(\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}S)\right)^{2}\right)\geq\frac{n^{-160\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{4}\text{.}

Therefore we have

\|A\|_{2}\leq 4\cdot\frac{n}{s}\cdot n^{160\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}=\frac{4\cdot n^{1+160\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{s}\text{.}

(108)

Since
G
is
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable by Lemma
22
for any vertex
x\in V
we have

\|m_{x}\|^{2}_{2}\leq O\left(k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)\text{.}

(109)

Then we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(m_{x})^{T}(M^{t}S)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{a\in I_{S}}\left((m_{x})^{T}(m_{a})\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{a\in I_{S}}\|m_{x}\|^{2}_{2}\|m_{a}\|_{2}^{2}}

By Cauchy Schwarz

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\sqrt{s\cdot\left(k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)^{2}}\right)

By (
109
)

\displaystyle=O\left(\sqrt{s}\cdot k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)

(110)

By the same analysis we get

\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}\leq O\left(\sqrt{s}\cdot k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)

(111)

Now we left to bound

(112)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\left(\frac{\sqrt{s}\cdot\xi}{c^{\prime}\cdot k^{2}\cdot n^{(1+200\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}\right)\left(\frac{4\cdot n^{1+160\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{s}\right)\cdot O\left(\sqrt{s}\cdot k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+(40\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)

Correctness:

Note that as per line

of Algorithm
4
we set

. Recall that
I_{S}=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}\}
is the multiset of
s
vertices each sampled uniformly at random (see line

of Algorithm
4
). Let
S
be the
n\times s
matrix whose

-th column equals

. Recall that

is the random walk transition matrix of

. Let

be the eigendecomposition of

. We define

e_{1}=\left|(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}S)\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})-\mathds{1}_{x}^{T}U_{[k]}{U}_{[k]}^{T}\mathds{1}_{y}\right|

and

e_{2}=\left|\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{x})^{T}(M^{t}S)\left(\frac{n}{s}\cdot\widetilde{W}_{[k]}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-4}_{[k]}\widetilde{W}^{T}_{[k]}\right)(M^{t}S)^{T}(M^{t}\mathds{1}_{y})\right|

By triangle inequality we have

\left|\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\langle f_{x},f_{y}\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\mathds{1}_{x}^{T}U_{[k]}{U}_{[k]}^{T}\mathds{1}_{y}\right|\leq e_{1}+e_{2}\text{.}

Let
\xi^{\prime}=\xi/2
. Let

in Lemma

and

be a constant in front of
s
in Lemma

. Note that as per line

Thus with probability at least

Space and runtime of
InitializeOracle
:

Algorithm
InitializeOracle
(
G,\delta,\xi
) (Algorithm
4
) samples a set
I_{S}
. Then as per line
6
of Algorithm
4
it estimates the empirical probability distribution of random walks starting from any vertex

for
O(\log n)
times. To that end as per line
2
of Algorithm
3
it runs
R_{\text{init}}
random walks of length
t
from each vertex
x\in I_{S}
. So it takes
O(\log n\cdot s\cdot R_{\text{init}}\cdot t)
time and requires
O(\log n\cdot s\cdot R_{\text{init}})
space to store endpoints of random walks. Then as per line
7
of Algorithm
4
it estimates matrix
\mathcal{G}
such that the entry corresponding to the
x^{\text{th}}
row and
y^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathcal{G}
is an estimation of pairwise collision probability of random walks starting from
x,y\in I_{S}
. To compute
\mathcal{G}
we call Algorithm
EstimateCollisionProbabilities
(
G,I_{S},R_{\text{init}},t
) (Algorithm
2
) for
O(\log n)
times. Algorithm
2
runs
R_{\text{init}}
random walks of length
t
from each vertex
x\in I_{S}
, hence, It takes
O(s\cdot R_{\text{init}}\cdot t\cdot\log n)
time and it requires

\mathcal{G}
. Then as per line

of Algorithm

we compute the SVD of matrix
\mathcal{G}
in time
O(s^{3})
. Thus overall Algorithm
4
runs in time
O\left(\log n\cdot s\cdot R_{\text{init}}\cdot t+s^{3}\right)
. Thus, by choice of
t=\varTheta\left(\frac{\log n}{\varphi^{2}}\right)
,
R_{\text{init}}=\varTheta{(n^{1-\delta+980\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot k^{17}/{\xi}^{2})}
and
s=\varTheta(n^{480\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log n\cdot k^{8}/{\xi}^{2})
as in Algorithm
4
we get that Algorithm

runs in time
O\left(\log n\cdot s\cdot R_{\text{init}}\cdot t+s^{3}\right)=(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1-\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\log^{3}n\cdot\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}
and returns a data structure of size
O\left(s^{2}+\log n\cdot s\cdot R_{\text{init}}\right)=(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1-\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\log^{2}n\text{.}

Space and runtime of

:

(

(Algorithm

) repeats

from vertex

and vertex

, then it computes

. Since

s

non-zero entries, thus one can compute

. Finally Algorithm

take entrywises median of computed vectors (see line

. Thus overall Algorithm

as in Algorithm

and Algorithm

we get that the Algorithm

and returns a data structure of size

∎

5.6
Computing approximate norms and spectral dot products (Proof of Theorem
6
)

To design the clustering algorithm in Section
6
, since we cannot evaluate the dot-product of the spectral embedding exactly in sublinear time, we prove that it is enough to have access to approximate dot-product of the spectral embedding.
In Algorithm
7
, Algorithm
9
and throughout the analysis of in Section
6
we will use
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
to denote approximate spectral dot products and
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
to denote the approximate norm of a vector. Let
r\in[k]
and
B,B_{1},\ldots,B_{r}\subseteq V
. Let
\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
where
\widehat{\mu}=\frac{\sum_{z\in B}f_{z}}{|B|}
and
\widehat{\mu}_{i}=\frac{\sum_{z\in B_{i}}f_{z}}{|B_{i}|}
. All dot products we will try to approximate in Section
6
will be of the form
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}(\widehat{\mu})\right\rangle
and all the norms that we approximate are of the form
\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}(\widehat{\mu})\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
, where
x\in V
and
\widehat{\Pi}
is defined as a orthogonal projection onto
span(\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}\})^{\perp}
. To compute such dot products we call Algorithm
6
in the following way (see Corollary
1
):

\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}:=\frac{1}{|B|}\cdot\sum_{y\in B}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\text{,}

(114)

\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|B|}\cdot\sum_{x\in B}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\text{.}

(115)

Algorithm 6

DotProductOracleOnSubspace
(
G,x,y,\delta,\xi,\mathcal{D},B_{1},\ldots,B_{r}
)
\triangleright
Need:
\epsilon/\varphi^{2}\leq\frac{1}{10^{5}}

\triangleright

\mathcal{D}:=\{\Psi,\widehat{Q}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{Q}_{O(\log n)}\}

1:

Let
X\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},h_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{r},h_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
.

2:

Let
\xi^{\prime}:=\Theta(\xi\cdot n^{(-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot k^{-6})

3:

for

i,j
in
[r]

do

4:

X(i,j):=\frac{1}{|B_{i}||B_{j}|}\cdot\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i}}\sum_{z_{j}\in B_{j}}\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,z_{i},z_{j},\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})

5:

\triangleright

X(i,j)=\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}

6:

for

i
in
[r]

do

7:

h_{x}(i):=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i}}\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,z_{i},x,\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})

\triangleright

h_{x}(i)=\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},f_{x}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}

8:

h_{y}(i):=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i}}\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,z_{i},y,\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})

\triangleright

h_{y}(i)=\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}

9:

return

\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}:=\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,x,y,\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})-h_{x}^{T}X^{-1}h_{y}

The following Lemma is a generalization of Lemma
14
to the approximation of the cluster means (i.e,
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}
), where
\widehat{\mu}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
is a vector that approximates the center of cluster
C_{i}
(i.e.,
\mu_{i}
) such that
||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{i}||_{2}
is small.

Lemma 30
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
, and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}
denote the cluster means of
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
0<\zeta<\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}
. Let
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
denote an approximation of the cluster means such that for each
i\in[k]
,
||\mu_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2}
. Let
S\subseteq\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
. Let
|S|=r
and
\widehat{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times r}
denote a matrix whose columns are the vectors in
S
. Let
\sigma:[r]\rightarrow[k]
denote a mapping from the the columns of
H
to the corresponding cluster. Let
\widehat{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
be a diagonal matrix such that
\widehat{W}(i,i)=\sqrt{|C_{\sigma(i)}|}
. Let
\widehat{Z}=\widehat{H}\widehat{W}
.Then for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

1.

|x^{T}(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z}-I)x|\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

2.

|x^{T}((\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}-I)x|\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\text{.}

Proof.

Proof of item (
1
)

Let
Y\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
be a matrix, whose
i
-th column is equal to
\sqrt{C_{i}}\cdot{\mu}_{i}
. By Lemma
9
item (
2
) for any vector
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with
||\alpha||_{2}=1
we have

|\alpha^{T}(Y^{T}Y-I)\alpha|\leq\frac{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

(116)

Let
\widehat{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
be a matrix, whose
i
-th column is equal to
\sqrt{C_{i}}\cdot\widehat{\mu}_{i}
. Note that for any
i,j\in[k]
we have
(Y^{T}Y)(i,j)=\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\left\langle\mu_{i},\mu_{j}\right\rangle
and
(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})(i,j)=\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle
. Therefore for any
i\in[k]
we have

\displaystyle\left|(Y^{T}Y)(i,i)-(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})(i,i)\right|

\displaystyle=|C_{i}|\left|||\mu_{i}||^{2}_{2}-||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||^{2}_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|C_{i}|\cdot|(||\mu_{i}||_{2}-||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||_{2})(||\mu_{i}||_{2}+||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq|C_{i}|\cdot\left|(\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2})(||\mu_{i}||_{2}+(1+\zeta)||{\mu_{i}}||_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\text{Since }||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||_{2}\leq(1+\zeta)||\mu_{i}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\cdot\zeta|C_{i}|\cdot||\mu_{i}||^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\text{Since }\zeta<1

\displaystyle\leq 6\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma \ref{lem:dotmu} }||\mu_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{2}{|C_{i}|}

Also for any
i\neq j\in[k]
we have

\displaystyle\left|(Y^{T}Y)(i,j)-(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})(i,j)\right|

(117)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\cdot\left|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mu_{i},\mu_{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\cdot\left|\left\langle{\mu}_{i}+(\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i}),{\mu}_{j}+(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j})\right\rangle-\left\langle{\mu}_{i},{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\cdot\left(|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle|+|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i},\mu_{j}\right\rangle|+|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j},\mu_{i}\right\rangle|\right)

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\cdot\left(||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i}||_{2}||\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}||_{2}+||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i}||_{2}||\mu_{j}||_{2}+||\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}||_{2}||\mu_{i}||_{2}\right)

By Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\cdot(\zeta^{2}+2\zeta)\left(||\mu_{i}||_{2}||\mu_{j}||_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\text{Since }||\widehat{\mu_{i}}-\mu_{i}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2}\text{ for all }i

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}\cdot 6\cdot\zeta\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma \ref{lem:dotmu} }||\mu_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{2}{|C_{i}|}\text{ for all }i

\displaystyle\leq 6\cdot\zeta

(118)

Therefore we have

\displaystyle||(Y^{T}Y)-(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq||(Y^{T}Y)-(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})||_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left((Y^{T}Y)(i,j)-(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})(i,j)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 6\cdot k\cdot\zeta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2\varphi}

Since
\zeta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}

Thus for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with
||\alpha||_{2}=1
we have

\left|\alpha^{T}\left((Y^{T}Y)-(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y})\right)\alpha\right|\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2\varphi}

(119)

Putting (
119
) and (
116
) together we get

\left|\alpha^{T}\left(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y}-I\right)\alpha\right|\leq 4.5\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
be a vector with
||x||_{2}=1
, and let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
be a vector that is
x_{j}=\alpha_{j}
if
\widehat{\mu}_{j}\in S
and otherwise
x_{j}=0
. Thus we have
||\alpha||_{2}=||x||_{2}=1
and
\widehat{Y}z=\widehat{Z}x
. Hence, we get

|x^{T}(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z}-I)x|=|\alpha^{T}(\widehat{Y}^{T}\widehat{Y}-I)\alpha|\leq\frac{4.5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Proof of item (
2
)

For any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

1-\frac{4.5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\leq x^{T}(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})x\leq 1+\frac{4.5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

(120)

Note that
\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z}
is symmetric and positive semidefinit. Also note that
\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z}
is spectrally close to
I
, hence,
\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z}
is invertible. Thus by (
120
) and Lemma
13
for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
we have

1-\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\leq x^{T}(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}x\leq 1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Therefore we get

|x^{T}((\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}-I)x|\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\text{.}

∎

Theorem 6
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\dots,C_{k}
. Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
,
\frac{1}{n^{5}}<\xi<1
, and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a positive absolute constant. Then there exists an event
\mathcal{E}
such that
\mathcal{E}
happens with probability
1-n^{-48}
and conditioned on
\mathcal{E}
the following holds.

Let
r\in[k]
. Let
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let
B_{1},\ldots,B_{r}
denote multisets of points. Let
b=\max_{i\in r}|B_{i}|
. Let
\sigma:[r]\rightarrow[k]
denote a mapping from the set
B
to the cluster
C=\sigma(B)
. Suppose that for all
i\in[r]
,
B_{i}\subseteq\sigma(B_{i})
and for all
i\neq j\in[r]
,
\sigma(B_{i})\neq\sigma(B_{j})
. Let
\widehat{\mu}_{i}=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot\sum_{z\in B_{i}}f_{z}
. Suppose that for each
i\in[r]
,
||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{\sigma(i)}||_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}||\mu_{i}||_{2}
. Let
\widehat{\Pi}
is defined as a orthogonal projection onto then
span(\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}\})^{\perp}
. Then for all
x,y\in V
we have

\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{\xi}{n},

where
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}:=\textsc{DotProductOracleOnSubspace}(G,x,y,\delta,\xi,\mathcal{D},B_{1},\ldots,B_{r})
.
Algorithm
6
runs in time
b^{2}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}
.

Proof.

Runtime:
Note that Algorithm
6
, first computes matrix
X\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, and vectors
h_{x},h_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
. To compute
X(i,j)
for any
i,j\in[r]
, as per line
4
of Algorithm
6
, we run
\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,z_{i},z_{j},\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})
for all
z_{i}\in B_{i}
and
z_{j}\in B_{j}
, where
|B_{i}|\leq b
and
|B_{j}|\leq b
.

Note that by Theorem
2
, Algorithm
\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,z_{i},z_{j},\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})
runs in time
(\frac{k}{\xi^{\prime}})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}
. Thus one can compute the matrix
X^{-1}
in time
O(k^{3}+k^{2}\cdot b^{2}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi^{\prime}})^{O(1)}n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}})
. Also, to compute
h_{x}(i)
(respectively,
h_{y}(i)
) for any
i\in[r]
, as per line
7
and line
8
of Algorithm
6
, we run
\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,x,z,\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})
for all
z\in B_{i}
(respectively,
z\in B_{j}
). Thus one can compute
h_{x}
and
h_{y}
in time
k\cdot b\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi^{\prime}})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}
. As per line (
2
) of Algorithm
6
we set
\xi^{\prime}:=\Theta(\xi\cdot n^{(-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot k^{-6})
.
Therefore the runtime of the algoritm is
b^{2}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}
.

Correctness:

Let
x,y\in V
. Let
H\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times r}
be a matrix whose columns are
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}
. Then we have
H\left(H^{T}H\right)^{-1}H^{T}
is the orthogonal projection matrix onto
span(\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}\})
. Let
W\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
denote a matrix such that for any
i\in[r]
,
W(i,i)=\sqrt{|C_{\sigma(i)}|}
. Note that

(HW)\left((HW)^{T}(HW)\right)^{-1}(HW)^{T}=HW\left(W^{-1}\left(H^{T}H\right)^{-1}W^{-1}\right)WH^{T}=H\left(H^{T}H\right)^{-1}H^{T}

Thus we have
(HW)\left((HW)^{T}(HW)\right)^{-1}(HW)^{T}
is the orthogonal projection matrix onto
span(\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}\})
and we get

\widehat{\Pi}=I-HW\left(WH^{T}HW\right)^{-1}WH^{T}

Therefore, we have

\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle=\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle-f_{x}^{T}HW\left(WH^{T}HW\right)^{-1}WH^{T}f_{y}

(121)

Let
\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}:=\textsc{SpectralDotProduct}(G,x,y,\delta,\xi^{\prime},\mathcal{D})
. Then as per line
9
of Algorithm
6
we have

\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}:=\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-h_{x}^{T}X^{-1}h_{y}\text{,}

(122)

where as per line (
4
) of Algorithm
6
for any
i,j\in[r]
we have
X(i,j)=\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
, and as per line (
7
) and line (
8
) of Algorithm
6
for any
i\in[r]
we have
h_{x}(i)=\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},f_{x}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
and
h_{y}(i)=\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
. Note that

h_{x}^{T}X^{-1}h_{y}=h_{x}^{T}WW^{-1}X^{-1}W^{-1}Wh_{y}=h_{x}^{T}W(WXW)^{-1}Wh_{y}

Therefore by (
122
), (
121
) and triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle\right|\leq|\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle|+\left|h_{x}^{T}W(WXW)^{-1}Wh_{y}-f_{x}^{T}HW\left(WH^{T}HW\right)^{-1}WH^{T}f_{y}\right|

Note that by Theorem
2
and by union bound over all pair of vertices with probability at least
1-n^{-100}\cdot n^{2}
for all
a,b\in V
we have

|\left\langle f_{a},f_{b}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{a},f_{b}\right\rangle|\leq\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{n}

(123)

We define

\mathbf{a}_{x}=f_{x}^{T}HW,\quad A=(WH^{T}HW)^{-1},\quad\mathbf{a}_{y}=WH^{T}f_{y}\text{, and}

\mathbf{e}_{x}=h_{x}^{T}W-\mathbf{a}_{x},\quad E=(WXW)^{-1}-A,\quad\mathbf{e}_{y}=Wh_{y}-\mathbf{a}_{y}

Thus by triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|h_{x}^{T}W(WXW)^{-1}Wh_{y}-f_{x}^{T}HW(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}WH^{T}f_{y}\right|=

\displaystyle\|\left(\mathbf{a}_{x}+\mathbf{e}_{x}\right)\left(A+E\right)\left(\mathbf{a}_{y}+\mathbf{e}_{y}\right)-\mathbf{a}_{x}A\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}

(124)

Thus we need to bound
||\mathbf{a}_{x}||_{2},||\mathbf{a}_{y}||_{2},||\mathbf{e}_{x}||_{2},||\mathbf{e}_{y}||_{2},||A||_{2},||E||_{2}
. Note that
||\mathbf{a}_{x}||_{2}=||f_{x}^{T}HW||_{2}
, Thus we have
||\mathbf{a}_{x}||_{2}\leq||f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}||W||_{2}
. Note that

||W||_{2}\leq\max_{i}W(i,i)=\max_{i}{\sqrt{|C_{i}|}}\leq\sqrt{n}

(125)

Then we bound
||f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}
.
Note that
||f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle}^{2}}
. We first bound
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle
.

\displaystyle\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot\sum_{z\in B_{i}}\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\sum_{z\in B_{i}}||f_{x}||_{2}||f_{z}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot\sum_{z\in B_{i}}\sqrt{k^{2}\cdot||f_{x}||^{2}_{\infty}||f_{z}||^{2}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot|B_{i}|\cdot k\cdot O\left(\frac{k\cdot n^{40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma \ref{lem:l-inf-bnd} and since }\min_{i\in k}|C_{i}|\geq\Omega\left(\frac{n}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O(k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}})

Since,
r<k
, we get

||f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle}^{2}}\leq\sqrt{k}\cdot O(k^{2}\cdot n^{-1+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}})\leq O(k^{2.5}\cdot n^{-1+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}})

(126)

Thus we get

||\mathbf{a}_{x}||_{2}=||f_{x}^{T}HW||_{2}\leq||f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}||W||_{2}\leq O\left(k^{2.5}\cdot n^{-1/2+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\right)

(127)

By the same computation we also have

||\mathbf{a}_{y}||_{2}\leq O\left(k^{2.5}\cdot n^{-1/2+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\right)

(128)

Next we bound
||\mathbf{e}_{x}||_{2}
. We have
\mathbf{e}_{x}=h_{x}^{T}W-f_{x}^{T}HW
. Thus we get
||\mathbf{e}_{x}||_{2}\leq||h_{x}^{T}-f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}||W||_{2}
. By (
125
) we have a bound on
||W||_{2}
.
Note that for any
i\in r
, we have
h_{x}(i)=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\sum_{z\in B_{i}}\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
and
(f_{x}^{T}H)(i)=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\sum_{z\in B_{i}}\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle
. Therefore with probability at least
1-n^{-98}
we have

\displaystyle|h_{x}(i)-(f_{x}^{T}H)(i)|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{b}\sum_{z\in B_{i}}(\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\sum_{z\in B_{i}}|\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},f_{z}\right\rangle|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot|B_{i}|\cdot\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{n}

By (
123
)

Since
r\leq k
, we have

||h_{x}^{T}-f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}(h_{x}(i)-\mathbf{a}_{x}(i))^{2}}\leq\sqrt{k}\cdot\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{n}

Therefore by (
125
) we have

||\mathbf{e}_{x}||_{2}\leq||h_{x}^{T}-f_{x}^{T}H||_{2}||W||_{2}\leq\frac{\xi^{\prime}\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{n}}

(129)

By the same computation we also have

||\mathbf{e}_{y}||_{2}\leq\frac{\xi^{\prime}\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{n}}

(130)

Next we bound
||A||_{2}
. Note that
A=((HW)^{T}(HW))^{-1}
. By Lemma
30
item (
2
) for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

\left|x^{T}\left(\left((HW)^{T}(HW)\right)^{-1}-I\right)x\right|\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Therefore

||A||_{2}=||((HW)^{T}(HW))^{-1}||_{2}\leq 1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\leq 2

(131)

Now we bound
||E||_{2}=||(WXW)^{-1}-(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}||_{2}
. For any
i,j\in[r]
we have

(WXW)(i,j)=\sqrt{|C_{\sigma(B_{i})}||C_{\sigma(B_{j})}|}\cdot\frac{1}{|B_{i}|\cdot|B_{j}|}\cdot\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i},z_{j}\in B_{j}}\left\langle f_{z_{i}},f_{z_{j}}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}

and

(WH^{T}HW)(i,j)=\sqrt{|C_{\sigma(B_{i})}||C_{\sigma(B_{j})}|}\cdot\frac{1}{|B_{i}|\cdot|B_{j}|}\cdot\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i},z_{j}\in B_{j}}\left\langle f_{z_{i}},f_{z_{j}}\right\rangle

Therefore with probability at least
1-n^{-98}
we have

\displaystyle|(WXW)(i,j)-(WH^{T}HW)(i,j)|

\displaystyle=\left|\sqrt{|C_{\sigma(B_{i})}||C_{\sigma(B_{j})}|}\cdot\frac{1}{|B_{i}|\cdot|B_{j}|}\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i},z_{j}\in B_{j}}(\hat{f}_{z_{i}z_{j}}-\left\langle f_{z_{i}},f_{z_{j}}\right\rangle)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|C_{\sigma(B_{i})}||C_{\sigma(B_{j})}|}\cdot\frac{1}{|B_{i}|\cdot|B_{j}|}\sum_{z_{i}\in B_{i},z_{j}\in B_{j}}|\hat{f}_{z_{i}z_{j}}-\left\langle f_{z_{i}},f_{z_{j}}\right\rangle|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\frac{1}{|B_{i}|\cdot|B_{j}|}\cdot|B_{i}|\cdot|B_{j}|\cdot\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{n}

\displaystyle\text{By \eqref{eq:fxhatfy} and since }|C|\leq n

(132)

Since
r\leq k
and by (
132
) we get

\displaystyle\left|||WXW-WH^{T}HW||_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq||WXW-WH^{T}HW||_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left((WXW)(i,j)-(WH^{T}HW)(i,j)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq k\cdot\xi^{\prime}

Thus for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

x^{T}(WH^{T}HW)x-k\cdot\xi^{\prime}\leq x^{T}(WXW)x\leq x^{T}(WH^{T}HW)x+k\cdot\xi^{\prime}

(133)

By Lemma
30
item (
1
) for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

|x^{T}\left((HW)^{T}(HW)-I\right)x|\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Hence we have

x^{T}(HW)^{T}(HW)x\geq 1-\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\geq\frac{1}{2}

(134)

Therfore by (
133
) and (
134
) we get for any vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

(1-2\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime})\cdot x^{T}(WH^{T}HW)x\leq x^{T}(WXW)x\leq(1+2\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime})\cdot x^{T}(WH^{T}HW)x

(135)

Note that
WH^{T}HW
is a symmetric matrix. Also note that by definition of
X
in line
4
of Algorithm
6
,
X
is a symmetric matrix, hence,
WXW
is symmetric and positive semidefinit. Also note that
WXW
is spectrally close to
WH^{T}HW
and
I
, hence,
WXW
is invertible. Thus by (
135
) and Lemma
13
we have

(1-4\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime})\cdot x^{T}(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}x\leq x^{T}(WXW)^{-1}x\leq(1+4\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime})\cdot x^{T}(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}x

Therefore by (
131
) we have

||E||_{2}=||(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}-(WXW)^{-1}||_{2}\leq 4\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime}\cdot||(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}||_{2}=8\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime}

(136)

Putting (
136
), (
131
), (
129
), (
130
), (
127
), (
128
) and(
124
) together, with probability at least
1-n^{-50}
we have

\displaystyle\left|h_{x}^{T}W(WXW)^{-1}Wh_{y}-f_{x}^{T}HW(WH^{T}HW)^{-1}WH^{T}f_{y}\right|=

\displaystyle\|\left(\mathbf{a}_{x}+\mathbf{e}_{x}\right)\left(A+E\right)\left(\mathbf{a}_{y}+\mathbf{e}_{y}\right)-\mathbf{a}_{x}A\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{a}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{a}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|A\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{e}_{x}\|_{2}\|E\|_{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{y}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\xi^{\prime}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot k^{2.5}\cdot n^{-1/2+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\right)+O\left(k\cdot\xi^{\prime}\cdot k^{5}\cdot n^{-1+80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+O\left(\xi^{\prime}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime}\cdot k^{2.5}\cdot n^{-1/2+40\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}\right)+O\left(\xi^{\prime 2}\cdot\frac{k}{n}\right)+O\left(\xi^{\prime 2}\cdot\frac{k}{n}\cdot k\cdot\xi^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\xi^{\prime}\cdot k^{6}\cdot n^{80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\xi}{n}

(137)

The last inequality holds by setting
\xi^{\prime}=\frac{\xi\cdot n^{(-80\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot k^{-6}}{c}
as per line of Algorithm
6
where
c
is a large enough constant to cancel the constant hidden in
O\left(\frac{\xi^{\prime}\cdot k^{6}\cdot n^{80\epsilon/\varphi^{2}}}{n}\right)
.

Therefore with probability at least
1-n^{-98}\geq 1-n^{-50}
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\widehat{f}_{xy}-\left\langle f_{x},f_{y}\right\rangle|+\left|h_{x}^{T}W(WXW)^{-1}Wh_{y}-f_{x}^{T}HW\left(WH^{T}HW\right)^{-1}WH^{T}f_{y}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{n}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\xi}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi}{n}

\displaystyle\text{By \eqref{eq:fxhatfy}, \eqref{eq:xi2-finn}, and since }\xi^{\prime}<\xi/2

(138)

Now let
\mathcal{E}
be the event that for all
x,y\in V
we have
|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}f_{y}\right\rangle|\leq\frac{\xi}{n}
. Then by (
138
) and the union bound we get that
\mathcal{E}
happens with probability at least
1-n^{-48}
and it is the claimed high probability event from the statement.

∎

Corollary 1
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\dots,C_{k}
. Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\frac{1}{n^{5}}<\xi<1
,
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a positive absolute constant. Let
\mathcal{E}
be the event that happens with probability
1-n^{-48}
that is guaranteed by Theorem
6
. Then conditioned on
\mathcal{E}
the following conditions hold.

Let
r\in[k]
. Let
B_{1},\ldots,B_{r},B^{\prime}
denote multisets of points. Let
b=\max\{|B_{1}|,\ldots,|B_{r}|,|B^{\prime}|\}
. Let
\sigma:[r]\rightarrow[k]
denote a mapping from the set
B
to the cluster
C=\sigma(B)
. Suppose that for all
i\in[r]
,
B_{i}\subseteq\sigma(B_{i})
and for all
i\neq j\in[r]
,
\sigma(B_{i})\neq\sigma(B_{j})
. Let
\widehat{\mu}_{i}=\frac{1}{|B_{i}|}\cdot\sum_{z\in B}f_{z}
for all
i\in[r]
, and let
\widehat{\mu}=\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{z\in B_{i}}f_{z}
. Suppose that for each
i\in[r]
,
||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{\sigma(i)}||_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}||\mu_{i}||_{2}
. Let
\widehat{\Pi}
is defined as a orthogonal projection onto then
span(\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{r}\})^{\perp}
. Then the following hold:

1.

There exits an algorithm that runs in time
b^{3}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}
and for any
x\in V
returns a value
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
such that

\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{\xi}{n}\text{.}

2.

There exits an algorithm that runs in time
b^{4}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}
and returns a value
\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}
such that
\left|\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}-||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}||_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\frac{\xi}{n}
.

Proof.

Proof of
1
:

To compute
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
we call Algorithm
6
,
b
times in the following way:

\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}:=\frac{1}{|B|}\cdot\sum_{y\in B}\textsc{DotProductOracleOnSubspace}(G,x,y,\delta,\mathcal{D},\xi,B_{1},\ldots,B_{r})

(139)

The runtime of Algorithm
6
is
b^{2}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot(\log n)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}
, thus the runtime of computation of
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
is
b^{3}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot(\log n)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}
. Moreover by Theorem
6
and the assumption that
\mathcal{E}
holds we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\sum_{y\in B^{\prime}}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}y\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\sum_{y\in B^{\prime}}\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}y\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\cdot|B^{\prime}|\cdot\frac{\xi}{n}

By Theorem
6

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi}{n}

Proof of
2
:

To compute
\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}
we call the procedure from item (
1
)
b
times in the following way:

\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}:=\frac{1}{|B|}\cdot\sum_{x\in B}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\text{.}

(140)

The runtime of the procedure from item (
1
) is
b^{3}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot(\log n)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}
, thus the runtime of computation of
\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}
is
b^{4}\cdot(\frac{k}{\xi})^{O(1)}\cdot n^{\delta+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot(\log n)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}
. Moreover by item (
1
) we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}-||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}||_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\left\langle\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{x\in B^{\prime}}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{x\in B^{\prime}}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{x\in B^{\prime}}\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}-\sum_{x\in B^{\prime}}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle\right|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B^{\prime}|}\cdot|B^{\prime}|\cdot\frac{\xi}{n}

By item (
1
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\xi}{n}\text{.}

∎

6
The main algorithm and its analysis

In this section we show that, by having access to approximate spectral dot-products for a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph
G
, we can assign each vertex in
G
to a cluster in sublinear time so that the resulting collection of clusters is, with high probability, a good approximation of a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering of
G
. In particular, we can show that the fraction of wrong assignments per cluster is at most
C\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{3}}\cdot\log(k)
, for some constant
C>0
. In the next subsection we describe our algorithm then in the remaining part of the section we present its analysis.

6.1
The Algorithm (Partitioning Scheme, Algorithm
7
)

We first present an idealized version of the sublinear clustering scheme defined by Algorithm
7
and Algorithm
10
. In this section to simplify presentation we assume
\varphi
to be constant.

The algorithm can be thought of as consisting of
3
parts. The first part, described in paragraph
Idealized Clustering Algorithm
, is a procedure that explicitly, in iterative fashion, produces a
k
-clustering of
G
. More precisely it recovers clusters in
O(\log(k))
stages, where for every
i
after the
i
-th stage at most
k/2^{i}
clusters are left unrecovered. The algorithm can be thought of as a version of carving of halfspaces in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
and it relies on the knowledge of cluster means
\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}
(recall that
\mu_{i}=\frac{1}{|C_{i}|}\sum_{x\in C_{i}}f_{x}
). That is why in paragraph
Finding approximate centers
we show how to compute approximations of
\mu_{i}
’s. To find good approximation to
\mu_{i}
’s we need to test many candidate sets
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
, which also means considering many candidate clusterings. This is a problem as we want our procedure to run in sublinear time but the idealized partitioning algorithm constructs clusterings explicitly! To solve this we explain in paragraph
Verifying a clustering
how to emulate the partitioning algorithm to test that, for a set of
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
, it indeed induces a good clustering.

Idealized Clustering Algorithm.

Assume that the we have access to cluster means
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
and dot product evaluations. The algorithm proceeds in
O(\log(k))
stages, in the first stage it considers
k
candidate sets
\widehat{C}_{i}
, where
x\in\widehat{C}_{i}
iff
f_{x}
has big correlation with
\mu_{i}
but small correlation with all other
\mu_{j}
’s. More precisely
x\in\widehat{C}_{i}
iff:

\left\langle f_{x},\mu_{i}\right\rangle\geq 0.93||\mu_{i}||^{2}\text{ and for all }j\neq i\left\langle f_{x},\mu_{j}\right\rangle<0.93||\mu_{j}||^{2}\text{.}

Note that by definition all these clusters are disjoint. Moreover we are able to show (see Lemma
37
) that at least
k/2
out of
\widehat{C}_{i}
’s are good approximate clusters, that is for each one of them there exists
j
such that
|\widehat{C}_{i}\triangle C_{j}|\leq O(\epsilon)\cdot|C_{j}|
. At this point we return these good clusters, remove the corresponding vertices from the graph, remove the corresponding
\mu
’s from the set
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
of still alive centers and proceed to the next stage.

In the next stage we restrict our attention to a lower dimensional subspace
\Pi
of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Intuitively we want to project out all the directions corresponding to the removed cluster centers. Recall that
\mu_{i}
’s are close to being orthogonal (see Lemma
12
and
7
) so projecting the returned directions out is almost equivalent to considering the subspace
\Pi:=\text{span}(\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{b}\})
, where
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{b}\}
is the set of still alive
\mu
’s. Now the algorithm considers
b
candidate clusters where the condition for
x
being in a cluster
i
changes to:

\left\langle f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\right\rangle\geq 0.93||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}\text{ and for all }j\in[b],j\neq i\left\langle f_{x},\Pi\mu_{j}\right\rangle<0.93||\Pi\mu_{j}||^{2}\text{.}

We are still able to show (also Lemma
37
) that at least
b/2
out of them are good approximate clusters. That is for each
i
there exists
j
such that
|\widehat{C}_{i}\triangle C_{j}|\leq O(\epsilon)\cdot|C_{j}|
but this time the constant hidden in the
O
notation is bigger than in the first stage. In general at any stage
t
the bound degrades to
O(\epsilon\cdot t)
. At the end of the stage we proceed in a similar fashion by returning the clusters, removing the corresponding vertices and
\mu
’s and considering a lower dimensional subspace of
\Pi
in the next stage.

The algorithm continues in such a fashion for
O(\log(k))
steps, as we guarantee that in each stage at least half of the remaining cluster means is removed. Thus the final guarantee is: there exists a permutation
\pi
on
k
elements such that for every
i
:

|\widehat{C}_{\pi(i)}\triangle C_{i}|\leq O\left(\epsilon\log(k)\right)\cdot|C_{i}|\text{.}

The decreasing (in the inclusion sense) sequence of subspaces
(\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\log(k)})
corresponds to the subspaces constructed in Algorithm
7
, while this offline algorithm as a whole corresponds to the sublinear Algorithm
10
that implicitly tries to construct a sequence of subspaces that (with respect to Algorithm
7
) defines a good clustering.

Finding approximate centers.

Note that cluster means are defined by the clustering, so it may seem that finding approximate means is a difficult operation. However, there is a relatively simple solution to this. In
Algorithm
10
we find approximate cluster means by sampling
O(\frac{\varphi^{2}}{\epsilon}k^{4}\log(k))
points, guessing cluster memberships and considering the means of the samples as cluster centers. We use that
the mean of a random sample of a cluster is typically close to the true mean of its cluster and so our sample means will provide a good estimation of the true means. We also remark that sampling a single vertex
from each cluster does not seem to provide a sufficiently good estimate, i.e. we require to take the mean of a sample
set
.

Verifying a clustering.

We also need a procedure that given an implicit sequence of subspaces
(\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\log(k)})
checks whether they indeed define (via Algorithm
7
) a good clustering. In fact, for every guess of cluster centers and the corresponding (as implicitly created by Algorithm
10
) sequence of
\Pi
’s we need to be able to check efficiently if the resulting clustering is a good approximation of a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering. Since we would like to do this in sublinear time as well,
we need to do this verification by random sampling. Then we design a procedure that consists of two steps. In a first step, we check if the cluster sizes are not too small. This is only a technical step, which is needed to
make sure that the later steps work. The main step is to test whether every cluster has small outer conductance (Algorithm
11
). In order to do so, we sample vertices uniformly at random and check
whether they are contained in the cluster that is currently checked. If this is the case, we sample a random edge incident to the sample vertex. This way, we obtain a random edge incident to a random vertex from the current
cluster (this follows since the conditional distribution is uniform over the cluster). We use standard concentration bounds to prove that we get a good approximation.

In the partitioning scheme and in the analysis a useful definition are subsets of vertices called threshold sets. A threshold set of a point
y
is the set of vertices with dot products (or approximate dot product) with
y
being above a specific threshold, more formally:

Definition 8

(
Threshold sets
)
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph (
as in Definition
2
). Recall that
f_{x}=F\mathds{1}_{x}
. For
y\in\mathbb{R}^{k},\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
we define:

C_{y,\theta}:=\{x\in V:\left\langle f_{x},y\right\rangle\geq\theta||y||^{2}\}

Definition 9

(
Approximate threshold sets
)
.

Let
G=(V,E)
be a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph (
as in Definition
2
). Recall that
f_{x}=F\mathds{1}_{x}
. For
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
such that
y=\widehat{\Pi}(\widehat{\mu})
, where
\widehat{\Pi}
is the orthogonal projection onto
span(\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{b}\})^{\perp}
and each
\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{b}
is an average of a set of embedded vertices:

C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{y,\theta}:=\{x\in V:\left\langle f_{x},y\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\geq\theta\left\lVert y\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}\}\text{.}

(141)

Recall that a discussion of how
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{apx}
and
\|\cdot\|_{apx}
are computed is presented in Section
5.6
.

Algorithm 7

HyperplanePartitioning
(
x,(T_{1},T_{2},\ldots,T_{b}))

\triangleright

T_{i}
’s are sets of
\widehat{\mu}_{j}
where
\widehat{\mu}_{j}
’s are given as sets of points

\triangleright
see Section
5.6
for the reason of such representation

1:

for

i=1
to
b

do

2:

Let
\Pi
be the projection onto the
\text{span}(\bigcup_{j<i}T_{j})^{\perp}
.

3:

Let
S_{i}=\bigcup_{j\geq i}T_{j}

4:

for

\hat{\mu}\in T_{i}

do

5:

if

x\in C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{\Pi\widehat{\mu},0.93}\setminus\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}^{\prime}\in S_{i}\setminus\{\widehat{\mu}\}}C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{\Pi\widehat{\mu}^{\prime},0.93}

then

\triangleright
see (
141
) for definition of
C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{y,\theta}

6:

return

\widehat{\mu}

HyperplanePartitioning
is the algorithm that, after preprocessing, is used to assign vertices to clusters. In the preprocessing step (see
ComputeOrderedPartition
in Section
6.3
) an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
of approximate cluster means
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
is computed.
HyperplanePartitioning
invoked with this ordered partition as a parameter induces a collection of clusters as follows:

Definition 10

(
Implicit clustering
)
.

For an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
of approximate cluster means
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
we say that
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
induces a collection of clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{k}}\}
if for all
i\in[k]
:

\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{i}}=\left\{x\in V:\textsc{HyperplanePartitioning}(x,(T_{1},\dots,T_{b}))=\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\}\text{.}

Remark 6
.

Ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
, precomputed in the preprocessing step (assuming access to
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
), will correspond to the
Idealized Clustering Algorithm
in the following sense. Number of sets in the partition (i.e.
b
) corresponds to the number of stages of
Idealized Clustering Algorithm
and for every
i\in[b]

T_{i}
contains exactly the
\mu
’s returned in stage
i
.

In the rest of this section we explain how to compute an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
of a set of approximate centers
(\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k})
such that the induced clustering
\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{k}}\}
satisfies that there exists a permutation
\pi
on
k
elements such that for all
i\in[k]
:

\left|\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{i}}\triangle C_{\pi(i)}\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{3}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C_{\pi(i)}|\text{.}

We start, in Subsection
6.2
, by studying geometric properties of our clustering instance. Recall, that we denote with
\mu_{i}
the center of cluster
C_{i}
in the spectral embedding. We show that, for specific choices of
\theta
, the threshold sets of
\mu_{i}
have large intersection with the cluster
C_{i}
and small intersections with all other cluster
C_{j}
. This fact intuitively suggests that our partitioning algorithm works. Unfortunately, as discussed in the technical overview, this is not enough to prove a per cluster guarantee. For this reason in Subsection
6.3
we analyze the overlap structure of
\{C_{\mu_{1},\theta},\dots,C_{\mu_{k},\theta}\}
more carefully and we give an algorithm (see
ComputeOrderedPartition
) that given real centers
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
and access to exact dot product evaluations computes an ordered partition of
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
that induces a valid clustering. In Subsection
6.4
we present an algorithm that guesses the cluster memberships for a set of randomly selected nodes and, using those guesses, approximates cluster centers. Interestingly, we can show, in Subsection
6.4.1
, that for the set of correct guesses the algorithm returns a good approximation of the cluster centers. Finally in Subsection
6.5
we show that we can find an ordered partition that induces a good clustering even if we have access only to approximate quantities. That is we show that even if we have access only to approximate means
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
and the dot product evaluations are only approximately correct then we can find an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
that induces a good collection of clusters. The last ingredient is to show that we are able to check if the clustering induced by a specific ordered partition is good. To solve this problem, we design an efficient and simple sampling algorithm which is also analyzed in Subsection
6.5
.

6.2
Bounding intersections of
C_{\mu_{i},\theta}
with true clusters
C_{i}

In this subsection we show that, for specific choices of
\theta
, the threshold sets of
\mu_{i}
(recall that
\mu_{i}
’s are cluster means in the spectral embedding) have large intersection with
C_{i}
and small intersections with other clusters. The main idea behind the proof is to use the bounds on dot product of cluster centers presented in Lemma
7
. In particular, we use Lemma
6
to relate
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
with the directional variance of the spectral embedding in the direction of
\mu_{i}
(i.e.
\sum_{x\in C_{i}}\langle f_{x}-\mu_{i},\alpha\rangle^{2}
). Then we use the definition of threshold set to upper and lower bound
\langle f_{x},\frac{\mu_{i}}{\|\mu_{i}\|}\rangle
and Lemma
7
to upper and lower bound the dot product between cluster centers. By combining the bounds we obtain the following result:

Lemma 31
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
. If
\mu_{i}
’s are cluster means then the following conditions hold. Let
S\subset\{{\mu}_{1},\dots,{\mu}_{k}\}
. Let
\Pi
denote the orthogonal projection matrix on to the
span(S)^{\perp}
. Let
\mu\in\{{\mu}_{1},\dots,{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus S
. Let
C
denote the cluster corresponding to the center
{\mu}
. Let

\widehat{C}:=\{x\in V:\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi{\mu}\right\rangle\geq 0.96\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}^{2}\}

then we have:

\left|C\setminus\widehat{C}\right|\leq\frac{10^{4}\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}|C|\text{.}

Proof.

Let
x\in C\setminus\widehat{C}
. Then:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mu-f_{x},\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\Pi({\mu}-f_{x}),\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\geq 0.04\cdot\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}

Since
\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi{\mu}\right\rangle<0.96\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 0.04\cdot\left(1-24\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\mu||_{2}

By Lemma
12

\displaystyle\geq 0.04\cdot\left(1-40\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{|C|}}

By Lemma
7

\displaystyle\geq 0.02\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{|C|}}

\displaystyle\text{Since }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ is sufficiently small}

Then by Lemma
6
applied to direction
\alpha=\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}
we have

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{x\in C_{i}}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{i},\alpha\right\rangle^{2}\leq\frac{4\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}.

On the other hand

\frac{4\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{x\in C_{i}}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{i},\alpha\right\rangle^{2}\geq\sum_{x\in C\setminus\widehat{C}}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu,\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}\right\rangle^{2}\geq 0.0004\cdot\frac{|C\setminus\widehat{C}|}{|C|}.

Using the above we conclude with
|C\setminus\widehat{C}|\leq 10^{4}\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}|C|
.
∎

Remark 7
.

Notice that the constants in Lemma
32
are different, they are equal
0.96
and
0.9
. The reason is that the real tests for membership in Algorithm
7
are performed with constant
0.93
and the slacks are needed as we have access only to approximate dot products. See (
214
) for the formal reason.

Lemma 32
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
. If
\mu_{i}
’s are cluster means then the following conditions hold. Let
S\subset\{{\mu}_{1},\dots,{\mu}_{k}\}
. Let
\Pi
denote the projection matrix on to
span(S)^{\perp}
. Let
\mu\in\{{\mu}_{1},\dots,{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus S
. Let
C
denote the cluster corresponding to the center
{\mu}
. Let

\widehat{C}:=\{x\in V:\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi{\mu}\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}^{2}\}

then we have:

\left|\widehat{C}\cap(V\setminus C)\right|\leq 100\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}|C|\text{.}

Proof.

Let
x\in\widehat{C}\cap(V\setminus C)
. Then there exists cluster
C^{\prime}\neq C
such that
x\in C^{\prime}
. Let
\mu^{\prime}
be the cluster mean of
C^{\prime}
. Then:

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f_{x}-\mu^{\prime},\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\geq\left|\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\frac{\Pi\mu}{\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|-\left|\left\langle\Pi\mu^{\prime},\frac{\Pi\mu}{\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}-\left|\left\langle\Pi\mu^{\prime},\frac{\Pi\mu}{\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

As
x\in\widehat{C}

Note that either
\mu^{\prime}\in S
and then
\Pi\mu^{\prime}=0
and in turn
|\left\langle\Pi\mu^{\prime},\Pi\mu\right\rangle|=0
or
\mu^{\prime}\not\in S
and then
|\left\langle\Pi\mu^{\prime},\Pi\mu\right\rangle|\leq\frac{60\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C|\cdot|C^{\prime}|}}
by Lemma
12
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f_{x}-\mu^{\prime},\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}-\frac{60\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C|\cdot|C^{\prime}|}}\frac{1}{\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq 0.8\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C|}}-\frac{120\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C|\cdot|C^{\prime}|}}\cdot\sqrt{|C|}

by Lemma
12
and Lemma
7
,
\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2\cdot\sqrt{|C|}}

\displaystyle\geq 0.2\sqrt{\frac{1}{|C|}}

\displaystyle\text{Since }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ sufficiently small and }\frac{|C|}{|C^{\prime}|}\text{ constant}

(142)

Then by Lemma
6
applied to direction
\alpha=\frac{\Pi{\mu}}{\|\Pi{\mu}\|_{2}}
we have

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{x\in C_{i}}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{i},\alpha\right\rangle^{2}\leq\frac{4\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}.

On the other hand using (
142
) we get

\frac{4\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{x\in C_{i}}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{i},\alpha\right\rangle^{2}\geq\sum_{x\in\widehat{C}\cap(V\setminus C)}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{x},\frac{\Pi{\mu_{x}}}{\|\Pi{\mu_{x}}\|_{2}}\right\rangle^{2}\geq 0.04\cdot\frac{|\widehat{C}\cap(V\setminus C)|}{|C|}.

Therefore we have
\left|\widehat{C}\cap(V\setminus C)\right|\leq 100\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}|C|
.
∎

6.3
Partitioning scheme works with
exact
cluster means & dot products

The goal of this section is to present the main ideas behind the algorithms and the analysis. In this section we make a couple of simplifying assumptions. We assume that:

•

We have access to real centers
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\},

•

Dot products computed by the algorithm are exact,

•

A test, that relies on computing outer-conductance of candidate sets, for assessing the quality of clusters is perfect.

Whenever we use one (or more) of these assumptions we state them explicitly in the Lemmas. Later in Section
6.5
we show that we can get rid of all of these assumptions.

In the previous section we showed geometric properties of the threshold sets. Recall that threshold sets are defined as follows:

C_{y,\theta}:=\{x\in V:\left\langle f_{x},y\right\rangle\geq\theta||y||^{2}\}\text{.}

In this section, using these properties of threshold sets, we show an algorithm that given exact centers, access to real dot products and a perfect primitive for computing outer-conductance computes an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
of
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
such that
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
induces a good collection of clusters.

Algorithm 8

ComputeOrderedPartition
(
G,\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k},s_{1},s_{2})

\triangleright

\widehat{\mu}_{i}
’s given as sets of points

\triangleright

s_{1}
is # sampled points for size estimation

\triangleright

s_{2}
is # of sampled points for conductance estimation

1:

S:=\{\hat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\hat{\mu}_{k}\}

2:

for

i=1
to
\lceil\log(k)\rceil

do

3:

T_{i}:=\emptyset

4:

for

\widehat{\mu}\in S

do

5:

\psi:=\textsc{OuterConductance}\left(G,\widehat{\mu},(T_{1},T_{2},\ldots,T_{i-1}),S,s_{1},s_{2}\right)

\triangleright
Algorithm
11

6:

if

\psi\leq O(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k))

then

7:

T_{i}:=T_{i}\cup\{\widehat{\mu}\}

8:

S:=S\setminus T_{i}

9:

if

S=\emptyset

then

10:

return

(\textsc{True},(T_{1},\dots,T_{i}))

11:

return

(\textsc{False},\bot)

To explain and analyze
ComputeOrderedPartition
we first need to introduce another algorithm and some definitions.

Definition 11
.

For a set
\{a_{1},\dots,a_{i}\}
we say a sequence
(S_{1},\dots,S_{p})
is an ordered partial partition of
\{a_{1},\dots,a_{i}\}
if:

•

\bigcup_{j\in[p]}S_{j}\subseteq\{a_{1},\dots,a_{i}\}
,

•

S_{i}
’s are pairwise disjoint.

Algorithm 9

IsInside
(
x,\widehat{\mu},(T_{1},T_{2},\ldots,T_{b}),S
)

\triangleright

T_{i}
’s are sets of
\widehat{\mu}_{j}
where
\widehat{\mu}_{j}
’s are given as sets of points

\triangleright
see Section
5.6
for the reason of such representation

\triangleright

S=
set of not yet processed centers,
\widehat{\mu}\in S

1:

for

i=1
to
b

do

2:

Let
\Pi
be the projection onto the
\text{span}(\bigcup_{j<i}T_{j})^{\perp}
.

3:

Let
S_{i}=\left(\bigcup_{j\geq i}T_{j}\right)\cup S

4:

for

\hat{\mu}_{i}\in T_{i}

do

5:

if

x\in C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{\Pi\widehat{\mu}_{i},0.93}\setminus\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}^{\prime}\in S_{i}\setminus\{\widehat{\mu}_{i}\}}C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{\Pi\widehat{\mu}^{\prime},0.93}

then

\triangleright
see (
141
) for definition of
C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{y,\theta}

6:

return

False

7:

Let
\Pi
be the projection onto the
\text{span}(\bigcup_{j\leq b}T_{j})^{\perp}
.

8:

if

x\in C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{\Pi\widehat{\mu},0.93}\setminus\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}^{\prime}\in S\setminus\{\widehat{\mu}\}}C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{\Pi\widehat{\mu}^{\prime},0.93}

then

\triangleright
see (
141
) for definition of
C^{\scriptscriptstyle apx}_{y,\theta}

9:

return

True

10:

return

False

Intuitively Algorithm
IsInside
emulates
ClassifyByHyperplanePartitioning
on ordered partial partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
. This intuition is made formal, after introducing Definition
12
, in Remark
8
. For this we need additional notation for clusters that are implicitly created by
IsInside
. We define:

Definition 12

(
Candidate cluster
)
.

For an ordered partial partition
P=(T_{1},\dots,T_{p})
of approximate cluster means
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
and
\widehat{\mu}\in\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in[p]}T_{i}
we say that
\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{P}
is a
candidate cluster corresponding to
\widehat{\mu}
with respect to

P
if:

\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{P}=\left\{x\in V:\textsc{IsInside}\left(x,\widehat{\mu},P,\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in[p]}T_{i}\right)=\textsc{True}\right\}\text{.}

Furthermore we define:

V^{P}:=V\setminus\bigcup_{j<p}\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}\in T_{j}}\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{j-1})}
.

Algorithm
IsInside
receives a vertex
x
, the centre of a cluster
\widehat{\mu}
, and an ordered partial partition, then it tests if vertex
x
is not recovered by any of the previous stages (see line (
5
) of Algorithm
9
) and can be recovered at the current stage using
\widehat{\mu}
. More formally, it can be recovered at the current stage if it only belongs to the candidate cluster corresponding to the center
\widehat{\mu}
(see line (
8
) of Algorithm
9
).

Remark 8
.

Note that Definitions
10
and
12
are compatible in the following sense. For an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
