of approximate cluster means
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
that induces a collection of clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{k}}\}
it is true that:

\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}_{k}}\}=\bigcup_{i\in[b]}\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}\in T_{i}}\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\}\text{,}

Equipped with Definition
12
we are ready to explain Algorithm
ComputeOrderedPartition
. The Algorithm proceeds in
O(\log(k))
stages. It maintains a set
S
of approximate cluster means, that initially is equal to
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
, from which
\widehat{\mu}
’s are removed after every stage. At every stage
i
a collection of sets

\mathcal{C}_{i}:=\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}\in S}\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\}\text{,}

is implicitly considered. In fact sets in this collection are, by definition, pairwise disjoint (see Defnition
12
and line:
8
of
IsInside
).
\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}
’s are defined as threshold sets (see Definition
8
) that are made disjoint by removing intersections. The main idea behind the Algorithm is to use properties from Section
6.2
so that we can show that
\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}
’s match some
C_{j}
’s well. Unfortunately after removing the intersections the above property might not hold for every cluster in
\mathcal{C}_{i}
. In the rest of this section we show however that it is true for a constant fraction of sets from
\mathcal{C}_{i}
. The Algorithm
ComputeOrderedPartition
proceeds by discarding, from set
S
, the
\widehat{\mu}
’s for which
\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}
matches some
C_{j}
’s well and implicitly removes the vertices of
\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}
from consideration. Moreover it projects out the directions corresponding to the removed
\widehat{\mu}
’s and restricts its attention to a lower dimensional subspace
\Pi
of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
(see
Idealized Clustering Algorithm
from Section
6.1
for comparison). The Algorithm doesn’t know which sets from
\mathcal{C}_{i}
are good as it runs in sublinear time. That is why we develop a simple sampling procedure that computes outer-conductance of candidate clusters (see Algorithm
11
). Then the Algorithm removes the
\widehat{\mu}
’s for which the corresponding
\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}
have small outer-conductance. We conclude using the robustness property of
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graphs (Lemma
16
) that these tests are enough.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to showing that if
ComputeOrderedPartition
is called with
(\hat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\hat{\mu}_{k})
equal to
(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k})
and the algorithm has access to real dot products then
ComputeOrderedPartition
returns
True
and an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
(of
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
) that induces a collection of pairwise disjoint clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\mu_{k}}\}
such that for every
i
:

\phi\left(\widehat{C}_{\mu_{i}}\right)\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)\text{.}

(143)

Then using Lemma
16
we get that there exists a permutation
\pi
such that for all
i\in[k]
:

\left|\widehat{C}_{\mu_{i}}\triangle C_{\pi(i)}\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{3}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C_{\pi(i)}|\text{.}

(144)

The core of the argument is an averaging argument that, for every linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
, bounds the average distance of embedded points to their centers in this subspace. What is important is that the bound depends linearly on the dimensionality of the subspace.

Lemma 33
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
.
Then for all
L\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{k}
- a linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\Pi
the orthogonal projection onto
L
we have:

\sum_{x\in V}\|\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq O\left(\text{dim}(L)\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)

Proof.

Let
b:=dim(L)
and
\{w_{1},\dots,w_{b}\}
be any orthonormal basis of
L
and recall that for
x\in V

\mu_{x}
is the cluster mean of the cluster which
x
belongs to. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in V}\|\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in V}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{x},w_{i}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{b}\sum_{x\in V}\left\langle f_{x}-\mu_{x},w_{i}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq b\cdot\frac{4\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}

By Lemma
6

∎

In order to show (
143
) we need to show that a constant fraction of candidate sets
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}
’s match some
C_{j}
’s well. To do that we argue that that sets of the form
C_{\Pi\widehat{\mu},0.9}
(where
\Pi
is the orthogonal projection onto the
\text{span}(\bigcup_{j<i}T_{j})^{\perp}
) don’t overlap too much. We do this in two steps. First in Lemma
34
and Lemma
35
we show that points from the intersections are far from their centers. Then in Lemma
36
below we show that having too many such vertices would contradict Lemma
33
.

Lemma 34
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
.
Let
\{v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
be a set of vectors satisfying:

•

|\left\langle v_{i},v_{j}\right\rangle|\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}

•

\left|||v_{i}||^{2}-\frac{1}{|C_{i}|}\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\frac{1}{|C_{i}|}

Then for every pair
i\neq j\in[k]
for every
\theta\in(0,1)
if
\alpha:=\frac{v_{i}\frac{\|v_{j}\|}{\|v_{i}\|}+v_{j}\frac{\|v_{i}\|}{\|v_{j}\|}}{\sqrt{\|v_{i}\|^{2}+\|v_{j}\|^{2}}}
and

I:=C_{v_{i},\theta}\cap C_{v_{j},\theta}=\{x\in V:\langle f_{x},v_{i}\rangle\geq\theta\|v_{i}\|^{2}\wedge\langle f_{x},v_{j}\rangle\geq\theta\|v_{j}\|^{2}\}

then the following conditions hold:

1.

Correlation of vector
v_{p}
with the direction
\alpha
is as follows:

•

\text{for all }p\in[k]\setminus\{i,j\},\left\langle\frac{\alpha}{\|\alpha\|},v_{p}\right\rangle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\frac{\|v_{i}\|\cdot\|v_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|v_{i}\|^{2}+\|v_{j}\|^{2}}}
, for all
i\neq j\in[k]

•

\text{for all }p\in\{i,j\},\left\langle\frac{\alpha}{\|\alpha\|},v_{p}\right\rangle\leq\left(1+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{\|v_{i}\|\cdot\|v_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|v_{i}\|^{2}+\|v_{j}\|^{2}}}
for all
i\in[k]

2.

Spectral embeddings of vertices from set
I
have big correlation with direction
\alpha
.

\min_{x\in I}\left\langle\frac{\alpha}{\|\alpha\|},f_{x}\right\rangle\geq\left(2\theta-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{\|v_{i}\|\cdot\|v_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|v_{i}\|^{2}+\|v_{j}\|^{2}}}

Proof.

For all
p\in[k]
let
\widetilde{v}_{p}:=v_{p}/||v_{p}||
. Let
\gamma:=\frac{||v_{j}||}{\sqrt{||v_{i}||^{2}+||v_{j}||^{2}}}
,
\alpha:=\gamma\widetilde{v}_{i}+\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\widetilde{v}_{j},
and
\widetilde{\alpha}:=\alpha/||\alpha||
. Fix
i\neq j\in[1,\dots,k]
. First we show that since
v_{i}
’s are close to orthogonal we have
||\alpha||^{2}\approx 1
. More precisely we will upper bound
|||\alpha||^{2}-1|

\displaystyle\left|||\alpha||^{2}-1\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\gamma^{2}||\widetilde{v}_{i}||^{2}+(1-\gamma^{2})||\widetilde{v}_{j}||^{2}+2\gamma\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\left\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{j}\right\rangle-1\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left\langle v_{i},v_{j}\right\rangle}{||v_{i}||^{2}+||v_{j}||^{2}}

\displaystyle\text{as }||\widetilde{v}_{i}||=||\widetilde{v}_{j}||=1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\cdot O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}}{\left(1-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)(\frac{1}{|C_{i}|}+\frac{1}{|C_{j}|})}

By assumptions

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\frac{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}{|C_{i}|+|C_{j}|}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)

\displaystyle\text{as }\frac{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}{\max(|C_{i}|,|C_{j}|)}\leq 1

(145)

Observe the following fact:

\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\cdot||v_{j}||=\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

(146)

Next notice the following:

\langle\alpha,v_{i}\rangle=\gamma||v_{i}||+\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{j}\rangle\cdot\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}||v_{i}||

(147)

\langle\alpha,v_{j}\rangle=\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{j}\rangle\cdot\gamma||v_{j}||+\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}||v_{j}||

(148)

For all
p\in\{1,2,\ldots,k\}\setminus\{i,j\}

\langle\alpha,v_{p}\rangle=\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{p}\rangle\cdot\gamma||v_{p}||+\left\langle\widetilde{v}_{j},\widetilde{v}_{p}\right\rangle\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}||v_{p}||

(149)

Moreover for all
p\neq q\in[1,\dots,k]
we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{||v_{p}||^{2}}\cdot\left\langle v_{q},v_{p}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{q}||C_{p}|}}|C_{p}|\frac{1}{\left(1-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)}

By assumptions

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\sqrt{\frac{|C_{p}|}{|C_{q}|}}

\displaystyle\text{for small enough }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)

\displaystyle\text{as }\frac{|C_{p}|}{|C_{q}|}=O(1)

(150)

Using the above we can prove:

\displaystyle\left|\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{j}\rangle\cdot\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}||v_{i}||\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\cdot||v_{j}||\cdot\frac{1}{||v_{j}||^{2}}\cdot\left\langle v_{i},v_{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\cdot||v_{j}||\cdot O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)

By (
150
)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

By (
146
)

(151)

And similarly we show:

\displaystyle\left|\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{j}\rangle\cdot\gamma||v_{j}||\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||\cdot\frac{1}{||v_{i}||^{2}}\cdot\left\langle v_{i},v_{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

By (
150
)

(152)

For all
p\in\{1,2,\ldots,k\}\setminus\{i,j\}
we get

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\alpha,v_{p}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\langle\widetilde{v}_{i},\widetilde{v}_{p}\rangle\cdot\gamma||v_{p}||\Big|+\left|\left\langle\widetilde{v}_{j},\widetilde{v}_{p}\right\rangle\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}||v_{p}||\right|

By (
149
)

\displaystyle=\left|\langle v_{i},v_{p}\rangle\cdot\frac{1}{||v_{i}||^{2}}||v_{i}||\gamma\right|+\left|\left\langle v_{j},v_{p}\right\rangle\frac{1}{||v_{j}||^{2}}||v_{j}||\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

By (
150
) and (
146
)

(153)

Combining (
147
), (
148
), (
151
), (
152
) and (
153
) we get that for all
p\in\{i,j\}
we have

\left\langle\alpha,v_{p}\right\rangle\leq\left(1+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

(154)

and
for all
p\in\{1,\dots,k\}\setminus\{i,j\}

\left\langle\alpha,v_{p}\right\rangle\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

(155)

Now using (
145
) we get that for all
p\in\{i,j\}

\left\langle\widetilde{\alpha},v_{p}\right\rangle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||\leq\left(1+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{||v_{i}||||v_{j}||}{\sqrt{||v_{i}||^{2}+||v_{j}||^{2}}}

and for all
p\in\{1,\dots,k\}\setminus\{i,j\}

\left\langle\widetilde{\alpha},v_{p}\right\rangle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)}}O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||\leq O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot\frac{||v_{i}||||v_{j}||}{\sqrt{||v_{i}||^{2}+||v_{j}||^{2}}}

These two inequalities establish the first statement of the Claim.

Recall that

I=\{x\in V:\langle f_{x},v_{i}\rangle\geq\theta||v_{i}||^{2}\wedge\langle f_{x},v_{j}\rangle\geq\theta||v_{j}||^{2}\}

Now let
x\in I
. Then observe

\displaystyle\left\langle\alpha,f_{x}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\gamma\cdot\widetilde{v}_{i},f_{x}\right\rangle+\left\langle\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\cdot\widetilde{v}_{j},f_{x}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\gamma\cdot\theta\cdot||v_{i}||+\sqrt{1-\gamma^{2}}\cdot\theta\cdot||v_{j}||

\displaystyle\text{because }x\in I

\displaystyle=2\theta\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

by (
146
)

Hence

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{\alpha},f_{x}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)}}2\theta\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

By (
145
)

\displaystyle\geq\left(2\theta-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\gamma\cdot||v_{i}||

∎

Now we use technical Lemma
34
to show that vertices from the intersections of
C_{\Pi\mu,0.9}
’s are far from their centers.

Lemma 35
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
.
If
\mu_{i}
’s are cluster means then the following conditions hold. For all
S\subset\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
if
L:=span(S)^{\perp}
and
\Pi
is the projection on
L
then if
x\in V
is such that

\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\wedge\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi\mu_{j}\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|_{2}^{2}

for some
\mu_{i},\mu_{j}\in\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}\setminus S,\mu_{i}\neq\mu_{j}
. Then:

\|\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}\|\geq 0.3\sqrt{\frac{1}{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}}

Proof.

Let
x\in V
be such that
\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi\mu_{j}\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Note that by Lemma
12
set
\{\Pi\mu_{1},\dots,\Pi\mu_{k}\}
satisfies assumptions of Lemma
34
. So applying Lemma
34
for
\theta=0.9
we get that there exists
\alpha\in\text{span}\{\Pi\mu_{i},\Pi\mu_{j}\},||\alpha||=1
such that:

•

\left\langle\alpha,f_{x}\right\rangle=\left\langle\alpha,\Pi f_{x}\right\rangle\geq(1.8-O(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}))\cdot\frac{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|\cdot\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}+\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|^{2}}}

•

\left\langle\alpha,\Pi\mu_{p}\right\rangle\leq(1+O(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}))\cdot\frac{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|\cdot\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}+\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|^{2}}}
, for all
p\in[k]

Thus we get

\displaystyle||\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}||

\displaystyle\geq|\left\langle\alpha,\Pi f_{x}\right\rangle-\left\langle\alpha,\Pi\mu_{x}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\geq\left(0.8-O\left(\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|\cdot\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}+\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|^{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq 0.75\cdot\frac{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|\cdot\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}+\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|^{2}}}

\displaystyle\text{By assumption that }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

(156)

without loss of generality we can assume
||\Pi\mu_{i}||\geq||\Pi\mu_{j}||
. Then we get:

\displaystyle\frac{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|\cdot\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|}{\sqrt{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}+\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|}{\sqrt{1+\|\Pi\mu_{j}\|^{2}/||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}||\Pi\mu_{j}||

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}}

\displaystyle\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:dosubspace}, assumption that }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

(157)

Combining (
156
) and (
157
) we get:

||\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}||\geq 0.3\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}}

∎

Combining Lemma
33
and Lemma
35
we show that sets
C_{\Pi\mu,0.9}
’s don’t overlap much.

Lemma 36
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
.
If
\mu_{i}
’s are cluster means then the following conditions hold. For all
S\subset\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
if
L:=span(S)^{\perp}
,
dim(L)=b
and
\Pi
is projection on
L
then:

\left|\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}\setminus S\\
\mu\neq\mu^{\prime}\end{subarray}}C_{\Pi\mu,0.9}\cap C_{\Pi\mu^{\prime},0.9}\right|\leq O\left(b\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{n}{k}\text{.}

Proof.

Let
x\in V
be such that
\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi\mu\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu\|_{2}^{2}
and
\left\langle\Pi f_{x},\Pi\mu^{\prime}\right\rangle\geq 0.9\|\Pi\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}
for some
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}\setminus S
. Then by Lemma
35
we get that

\|\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}\|\geq 0.3\sqrt{\frac{1}{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}}\text{.}

(158)

On the other hand Lemma
33
guarantees:

\sum_{x\in V}\|\Pi f_{x}-\Pi\mu_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq O\left(dim(L)\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)

(159)

Combining (
158
), (
159
) and the fact that
\frac{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}=O(1)
we get

\left|\bigcup_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}\setminus S}C_{\Pi\mu,0.93}\cap C_{\Pi\mu^{\prime},0.93}\right|\leq O\left(b\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{n}{k}

∎

Our bounds above enable the following analysis. At every stage of the for loop from line
4
of Algorithm
8
at least half of the candidate clusters:

\mathcal{C}_{i}:=\bigcup_{\widehat{\mu}\in S}\{\widehat{C}_{\widehat{\mu}}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\}\text{,}

passes the test from line
6
of Algorithm
8
, which means that they have small outer-conductance and satisfy condition (
143
).

Lemma 37
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
.

If
ComputeOrderedPartition
(
G,\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k},s_{1},s_{2})
is invoked with
(\hat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\hat{\mu}_{k})=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k})
and we assume that all tests Algorithm
8
performs
\left(\textit{i.e.}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}0.93\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}\right)
are exact and
OuterConductance
computes outer-conductance precisely then there exists an absolute constant
\Upsilon
such that the following conditions hold.

For any
i\in[0..\log(k)]
assume that at the beginning of the
i
-th iteration of the for loop from line
4
of Algorithm
8

|S|=b
and, up to renaming of
\mu
’s,
S=\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{b}\}
, the corresponding clusters are
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\dots,C_{b}\}
respectively and the ordered partial partition of
\mu
’s is equal to
(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})
.
Then if for every
C\in\mathcal{C}
we have that
|V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
then at the beginning of
(i+1)
-th iteration:

1.

|S|\leq b/2
(that is at least half of the remaining cluster means were removed in
i
-th iteration),

2.

for every
\mu\in S
the corresponding cluster
C
satisfies
|V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i})}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot(i+1)\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
, where
(T_{1},\dots,T_{i})
is the ordered partial partition of
\mu
’s created in the first
i
iterations.

Proof.

Let
i\in[0..\log(k)]
, without loss of generality we can assume that
S=\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{b}\}
(if not we can rename the
\mu
’s) at the beginning of the
i
-th iteration and the corresponding clusters be
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\dots,C_{b}\}
respectively. Assume that for every
C\in\mathcal{C}
we have that
|V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
. We start by showing the first part of the Lemma.

At least half of the cluster means is removed from
S
:

Let
\mu\in S
,
\Pi_{i}
be the orthogonal projection onto the
\text{span}(\bigcup_{j<i}T_{j})^{\perp}
, where
(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})
is the ordered partial partition of
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
created before iteration
i
by
ComputeOrderedPartition
. For brevity we will refer to
(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})
as
P
in this proof. Let

I:=\bigcup_{\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime}\in\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{b}\}}C_{\Pi_{i}\mu^{\prime},0.93}\cap C_{\Pi_{i}\mu^{\prime\prime},0.93}\text{.}

By Lemma
36
we have that

\left|I\right|\leq O\left(b\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{n}{k}

So by Markov inequality we get that there exists a subset of clusters
\mathcal{R}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
such that
|\mathcal{R}|\geq b/2
and for every
C\in\mathcal{R}
we have that

|C\cap I|\leq 2\cdot O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\cdot\frac{n}{k}

(160)

We will argue that for any order of the for loop from line
4
of Algorithm
8
it is true that for every
C\in\mathcal{R}
with corresponding mean
\mu
the
candidate cluster
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
satisfies the if statement from line
6
.

First note that behavior of the algorithm is independent of the order of the for loop from line
4
of Algorithm
8
as by definition
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
’s for
\mu\in S
are pairwise disjoint. Now let
C\in\mathcal{R}
,
\mu
be the corresponding mean to
C
and
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
be the candidate cluster corresponding to
\mu
with respect to
P=(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})
. By inductive assumption
|V^{P}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
so by (
160
), Lemma
31
and the fact that
\frac{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}=O(1)
we get that:

\displaystyle|\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}\cap C|

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|-O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\frac{n}{k}-O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)\right)|C|

(161)

To prove that
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
passes the outer-conductance test we also need to show that
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
doesn’t contain a lot of points from
V^{P}\setminus C
. By Lemma
32
we get that:

|\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}\cap(V^{P}\setminus C)|\leq|\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}\cap(V\setminus C)|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|\text{.}

(162)

Combining (
162
) and (
161
) we get that:

|\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}\triangle C|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C|

(163)

Now we want to argue that
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
passes the outerconductance test from line
6
of Algorithm
8
. From the definition of outer conductance:

\displaystyle\phi(\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{E(C,V\setminus C)+d|\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}\triangle C|}{d(|C|-|\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}\triangle C|)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{E(C,V\setminus C)+d\cdot O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C|}{d(|C|-O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C|)}

from (
163
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)}{1-O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)}

\displaystyle\text{because }\frac{E(C,V\setminus C)}{d|C|}\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)

\displaystyle\text{for sufficiently small }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)

and it follows that

\phi(\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P})\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)\text{,}

which means that
\widehat{C}_{\mu}^{P}
passes the test as we assumed that
OuterConductance
computes outer-conductance precisely.

Clusters corresponding to unremoved
\mu
’s satisfy condition
2
:

Now we prove that for every
\mu
that was not removed from set
S
only small fraction of its corresponding cluster is removed.

Let
\mu\in S
be such that it is not removed in the
i
-th step. Let
\Pi_{i}
be the orthogonal projection onto the
\text{span}(\bigcup_{j<i}T_{j})^{\perp}
. Let
C\in\mathcal{C}
be the cluster corresponding to
\mu
. By assumption
|V^{P}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
. Now let
x\in V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\setminus V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i})}
, where
(T_{1},\dots,T_{i})
is the partial partition of
\mu
’s created in the first
i
-th steps of the for loop. We get that there exists
\mu^{\prime}\in\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{b}\}
such that
x\in\widehat{C}_{\mu^{\prime}}^{P}
(recall that
\widehat{C}_{\mu^{\prime}}^{P}
is the candidate cluster corresponding to
\mu^{\prime}
with respect to
P=(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})
). Recall (Definition
12
) that
\widehat{C}_{\mu^{\prime}}^{P}
is defined as:

\widehat{C}_{\mu^{\prime}}^{P}=\left\{x\in V:\textsc{IsInside}\left(x,\mu^{\prime},P,\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}\setminus\bigcup_{j\in[i-1]}T_{j}\right)=\textsc{True}\right\}\text{.}

This in particular means (see line
8
: of Algorithm
IsInside
) that:

\widehat{C}_{\mu^{\prime}}^{P}\subseteq C_{\Pi_{i}\mu^{\prime},0.93}\setminus\bigcup_{\mu^{\prime\prime}\in S\setminus\{\mu^{\prime}\}}C_{\Pi_{i}\mu^{\prime\prime},0.93},

which, as
\mu\in S\setminus\{\mu^{\prime}\}
, gives us that:

\widehat{C}_{\mu^{\prime}}^{P}\cap C_{\Pi_{i}\mu,0.93}=\emptyset\text{,}

and finally, using Definition
8
, we have:

\left\langle f_{x},\Pi_{i}\mu\right\rangle<0.93||\Pi_{i}\mu||^{2}\text{.}

(164)

But by Lemma
31
:

|\{x\in C:\left\langle\Pi_{i}f_{x},\Pi_{i}\mu\right\rangle<0.93\|\Pi_{i}\mu\|_{2}^{2}\}|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\cdot|C|

(165)

Combining (
164
) and (
165
) we get that:

|C\cap(V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\setminus V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i})})|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|\text{.}

(166)

By assumption that
|V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
and (
166
) we get that:

|V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i})}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot(i+1)\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|\text{,}

provided that
\Upsilon
is bigger than the constant from
O
notation in (
166
), which is the same constant as the one in the statement of Lemma
31
.

∎

Remark 9
.

Note that in this section we assume that the Algorithm has access to real centers
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
. If it was the case in the final algorithm we could in fact prove a stronger guarantee, i.e. ”Algorithm
8
returns
True
and an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
(of
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
) that induces a collection of pairwise disjoint clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\mu_{k}}\}
such that there exists a permutation
\pi
such that for all
i\in[k]
:

\left|\widehat{C}_{\mu_{i}}\triangle C_{\pi(i)}\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C_{\pi(i)}|\text{\text{".}}

Compare the above statement with with (
144
) and the main theorem of this section, Theorem
7
. The reason we present it this way is the following.

The final algorithm doesn’t have access to
\mu
’s but instead tests many candidate sets
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
. Moreover Algorithm
8
returns an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
that induces a collection of clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{1},\dots,\widehat{C}_{k}\}
whenever every set from this collection passes the test from line
6
of
ComputeOrderedPartition
, that is when for every
\widehat{C}\in\{\widehat{C}_{1},\dots,\widehat{C}_{k}\}
:

\phi\left(\widehat{C}\right)\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)\text{.}

This in particular means that Algorithm
8
may return
True
even for a set
\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
that is
not
a good approximation to
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
.

Because of that, once we know that
ComputeOrderedPartition
invoked with
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
returns an ordered partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
that induces a collection of clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{1},\dots,\widehat{C}_{k}\}
, when proving the final result of this section (Theorem
7
) the only thing we assume about
\widehat{C}
’s is that they passed the outer-conductance test. And that is why we use Lemma
16
and we ”loose” a factor
\frac{1}{\varphi}
in the final guarantee.

Moreover structuring the argument in this way helps the presentation as later, in Section
6.5
, the proof will follow a similar structure.

The following Theorem concludes this subsection by showing (
144
). It does so by induction using Lemma
37
as an inductive step. At the end it uses Lemma
16
to go from the guarantees for outer-conductance to guarantees for recovery.

Theorem 7
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\log(k)
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
.

If
ComputeOrderedPartition
(
G,\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k},s_{1},s_{2})
is invoked with
(\hat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\hat{\mu}_{k})=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k})
and we assume that all tests Algorithm
8
performs
\left(\textit{i.e.}\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\geq}}0.93\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}\right)
are exact and
OuterConductance
computes outer-conductance precisely then the following conditions hold.

ComputeOrderedPartition
returns
(\textsc{True},(T_{1},\dots,T_{b}))
such that
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
induces a collection of clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\mu_{k}}\}
such that there exists a permutation
\pi
on
k
elements such that for all
i\in[k]
:

\left|\widehat{C}_{\mu_{i}}\triangle C_{\pi(i)}\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{3}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C_{\pi(i)}|

and

\phi(\widehat{C}_{\mu_{i}})\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)\text{.}

Proof.

Note that for
i=0
in the for loop in line
2
of
ComputeOrderedPartition

S
and clusters
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}
trivially satisfy assumptions of Lemma
37
. So using Lemma
37
and induction we get that for every
i\in[0..\lceil\log(k)\rceil]
at the beginning of the
i
-th iteration:

•

|S|\leq k/2^{i}
,

•

for every
\mu\in S
and the corresponding cluster
C
we have
|V^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{i-1})}\cap C|\geq\left(1-\Upsilon\cdot i\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\right)|C|
(where
\Upsilon
is the constant from the statement of Lemma
37
).

In particular this means that after at most
\lceil\log(k)\rceil
iterations set
S
becomes empty. This also means that
ComputeOrderedPartition
returns in line
10
, so it returns
True
and the ordered partial partition
(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})
is in fact an ordered partition of
\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
.

Note that by definition (see Definition
10
) all the approximate clusters
\{\widehat{C}_{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\mu_{k}}\}
are pairwise disjoint and moreover for every constructed cluster
\widehat{C}\in\{\widehat{C}_{\mu_{1}},\dots,\widehat{C}_{\mu_{k}}\}
we have:

\phi(\widehat{C})\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\cdot\log(k)\right)\text{,}

as it passed the test in line
6
of
ComputeOrderedPartition
. So by Lemma
16
it means that there exists a permutation
\pi
on
k
elements such that for all
i\in[k]
:

\left|\widehat{C}_{\mu_{i}}\triangle C_{\pi(i)}\right|\leq O\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{3}}\cdot\log(k)\right)|C_{\pi(i)}|\text{.}

∎

6.4
Finding the cluster means

In the previous subsection we showed that
ComputeOrderedPartition
succeeds if we have access to real cluster centers (i.e.
\mu_{i}
’s). In this section we present a search procedure for finding the centers.

The main idea behind our algorithm is to guess the clustering assignment of few random nodes and use this assignment to compute the approximate cluster means. More precisely, the first step of our algorithm is to learn the spectral embedding as described in Section
5
. Then we sample
s=\Omega(\frac{\varphi^{2}}{\epsilon}\cdot k^{4}\log(k))
random nodes and we consider all the possible clustering assignments for them. For each assignment, we implicitly define the cluster center for a specific cluster as
\widehat{\mu}_{i}:=\frac{1}{|P_{i}|}\sum_{x\in P_{i}}f_{x}
.

Remark 10
.

We note that in
FindCenters
we don’t necessarily find
\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}
exactly but we are able to show (see Section
6.4.1
) that it finds a good approximation to
\mu_{i}
’s. Then in Section
6.5
we show that such approximation is sufficient for the partitioning scheme to work.

Algorithm 10

FindCenters
(
G,\eta,\delta)

1:

\textsc{InitializeOracle}(G,\delta)

2:

for

t\in[1\dots{\log(2/\eta)}]

do

3:

S:=
Random sample of vertices of
V
of size
s=\varTheta(\frac{\varphi^{2}}{\epsilon}k^{4}\log(k))

4:

for

(P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{k})\in\textsc{Partitions}(S)

do

5:

for

i=1
to
k

do

6:

\widehat{\mu}_{i}:=\frac{1}{|P_{i}|}\sum_{x\in P_{i}}f_{x}

\triangleright
Note that we compute the centers only implicitly.

7:

(r,C):=

8:

ComputeOrderedPartition
\left(G,(\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}),\Theta\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}}{\epsilon}k^{5}\log^{2}(k)\log(1/\eta)\right),\Theta\left(\frac{\varphi^{4}}{\epsilon^{2}}k^{5}\log^{2}(k)\log(1/\eta)\right)\right)

9:

if

r=

True

then

10:

return

C

6.4.1
Quality of cluster means approximation

In the previous Section
6.3
we showed that the partitioning scheme works if we can find
\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}
exactly. In this section we show that it is possible to estimate the cluster means with a small error factor (i.e
\mu_{i}\approx\widehat{\mu}_{i}
). Later in Section
6.5
we show that such an approximation to
\mu_{i}
’s is enough for the partitioning scheme to work.

In the rest of this section we show that if
\textsc{Partitions}(S)
(see Algorithm
10
) computes a correct guess of cluster assignments then the cluster means computed in line (
6
) are close to the real cluster means with constant probability. Then we repeat the procedure
O(\log(1/\eta))
times to achieve success probability of at least
1-\eta
.

In particular, in Lemma
39
we show using Matrix Bernstein that if we have enough samples in a cluster
i
then
\|\mu_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\zeta\cdot\|\mu_{i}\|_{2}
. Then we prove that if we sample enough random nodes we have enough samples in every cluster.

Before proving Lemma
39
we show a tail bound for the spectral projection of a node that will be useful to apply Matrix Bernstein.

Lemma 38
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
and
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\log(k)
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular and a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clusterable graph. Let
\beta>1
.Let

T=\left\{x\in V:||f_{x}||_{\infty}\geq\beta\cdot\sqrt{\frac{10}{\min_{i\in[k]}|C_{i}|}}\right\}\text{.}

Then we have
|T|\leq k\cdot\left(\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{-\varphi^{2}/20\cdot\epsilon}\cdot(\min_{i\in[k]}|C_{i}|)
.

Proof.

Recall that
f_{x}=U_{[k]}^{T}\mathds{1}_{x}
, and
u_{i}
denote the
i^{\text{th}}
column of
U_{[k]}
. Thus we have
\|f_{x}\|_{\infty}=\max_{i\in[k]}\{u_{i}(x)\}
. Let
s_{\min}=\min_{i\in k}|C_{i}|
. We define

T_{i}=\left\{x\in V:|u_{i}(x)|\geq\beta\cdot\sqrt{\frac{10}{s_{\min}}}\right\}

Therefore, by Lemma
4
we have
|T_{i}|\leq\left(\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{-{\varphi^{2}/20\cdot\epsilon}}\cdot s_{\min}\text{.}
Note that
T=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}T_{i}
. Therefore we have

|T|\leq k\cdot\left(\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{-{\varphi^{2}/20\cdot\epsilon}}\cdot s_{\min}

∎

Now we are ready to derive a bound on the difference between
\mu_{i}
and
\widehat{\mu}_{i}
.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\zeta,\delta\in(0,1)
,
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
,
\frac{\epsilon\log k}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a positive sufficiently small constant. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
s\geq c\cdot\left(k\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/(1-(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}))}
for large enough constant
c
. Let
S=\{x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}\}
be the multiset with
s
vertices sampled uniformly at random from cluster
C
. Let
\mu=\frac{1}{|C|}\sum_{x\in C}f_{x}
denote the cluster mean, and let
\widehat{\mu}=\frac{1}{s}\sum_{i=1}^{s}f_{x}
denote the empirical cluster mean. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
we have

\|\mu-\widehat{\mu}\|_{2}\leq\zeta\cdot\|\mu\|_{2}

Proof.

Let
s_{\min}:=\min_{i\in[k]}|C_{i}|
. We define

C^{\prime}=\left\{x\in C:||f_{x}||_{\infty}\leq 2\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{10}{s_{\min}}}\right\}

Note that by Lemma
38
and by choice of
\beta=2\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}
we have

|C\setminus C^{\prime}|\leq k\cdot\left(\frac{\beta}{2}\right)^{-\varphi^{2}/(20\cdot\epsilon)}\cdot s_{\min}\leq k\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{-2}\cdot|C|=(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})\cdot|C|

Thus we have

|C^{\prime}|\geq\left(1-(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})\right)|C|

(167)

Let
\mu^{\prime}=\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C^{\prime}}f_{x}
. By triangle inequality we have

||\widehat{\mu}-\mu||_{2}\leq||\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\prime}||_{2}+||\mu^{\prime}-\mu||_{2}

(168)

In the rest of the proof we will upper bound both of these terms by
\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}
.

Step
1
:
We first prove
||\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\prime}||_{2}\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}
. By the assumption of the lemma for sufficiently small
\frac{\epsilon\log k}{\varphi^{2}}
we have
k^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\leq 2
. Thus for any
x\in C^{\prime}
we have
||f_{x}||_{\infty}\leq\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{160}{s_{\min}}}
. Therefore by triangle inequality we have

||\mu^{\prime}||_{2}=\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{x\in C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|\leq\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{x\in C^{\prime}}||f_{x}||_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}}{|C^{\prime}|}\cdot\sum_{x\in C^{\prime}}||f_{x}||_{\infty}\leq\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{160\cdot k}{s_{\min}}}\text{.}

(169)

By (
167
) and by union bound over all samples in
S
with probability at least
1-s\cdot(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})=1-s^{-1}\cdot k^{-1}\cdot\delta^{2}\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2}
for all
x_{i}\in S
we have
x_{i}\in C^{\prime}
, hence,
||f_{x}||_{\infty}\leq\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{160}{s_{\min}}}
. Thus with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
,
S
is chosen uniformly at random from
C^{\prime}
so for all
x_{i}\in S
we have

|f_{x}||_{\infty}\leq\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{160}{s_{\min}}}

(170)

In the rest of the proof of step
1
we assume
S\subseteq C^{\prime}
which holds with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
. Therefore conditioned on
S\subseteq C^{\prime}
we have
\mathbb{E}[f_{x_{i}}]=\mu^{\prime}
.

\|\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}=\left|\left|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\frac{f_{x_{i}}}{s}-\mu^{\prime}\right)\right|\right|_{2}\text{.}

We define
\mathbf{z}_{i}=\frac{f_{x_{i}}}{s}-\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{s}
, so
\|\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}=\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathbf{z}_{i}\|_{2}
. Observe that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{z}_{i}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{f_{x_{i}}}{s}\right]-\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{s}=0
, thus we can apply Lemma
20
. Therefore we get

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\left|\widehat{\mu}-{\mu^{\prime}}\right|\right|_{2}>q\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathbf{z}_{i}\|_{2}>q\right]\leq(k+1)\cdot\text{exp}\left(\frac{\frac{-q^{2}}{2}}{\sigma^{2}+\frac{bq}{3}}\right)\text{,}

(171)

where
\sigma^{2}=\max\{\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{T}]\|_{2},\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{i}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{i}]\|_{2}\}
and
b
is an upper bound on
\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|_{2}
for all random variables
\mathbf{z}_{i}
. Therefore we need to upperbound
\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|_{2}
and
\sigma^{2}
. Note that

\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|_{2}=\left|\left|\frac{f_{x_{i}}}{s}-\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{s}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\left|\left|\frac{f_{x_{i}}}{s}\right|\right|_{2}+\left|\left|\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{s}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}}{s}\cdot\|f_{x_{i}}\|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{s}\cdot||\mu^{\prime}||_{2}

(172)

Therefore by (
169
), (
170
) and (
172
) we have

\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}}{s}\cdot\|f_{x_{i}}\|_{\infty}+\frac{1}{s}\cdot||\mu^{\prime}||_{2}\leq\frac{2}{s}\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{160\cdot k}{s_{\min}}}\text{,}

(173)

Thus
b\leq\frac{2}{s}\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{160\cdot k}{s_{\min}}}
. We also need to upper bound
\sigma^{2}
. By (
173
) we get

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\max\{\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{T}]\|_{2},\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{i}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{i}]\|_{2}\}=s\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{z}_{i}\|^{2}_{2}\right]\leq s\cdot\frac{4}{s^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\frac{160\cdot k}{s_{\min}}\text{.}

(174)

We set
q=\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}
.
Having upper bound for
\sigma^{2}
by (
174
) and on
b
by (
173
) we can apply Lemma
20
and we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\left|\widehat{\mu}-{\mu^{\prime}}\right|\right|_{2}>\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(k+1)\cdot\text{exp}\left(\frac{\frac{-q^{2}}{2}}{\sigma^{2}+\frac{bq}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(k+1)\cdot\text{exp}\left(\frac{\frac{-\zeta^{2}\cdot||\mu||^{2}_{2}}{8}}{\frac{640\cdot k\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{s\cdot s_{\min}}+\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}\cdot\frac{2\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{3\cdot s}\sqrt{\frac{160\cdot k}{s_{\min}}}}\right)

(175)

By Lemma
7
for small enough
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
we have
\|\mu\|^{2}_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2\cdot|C|}
and since
\min_{i,j}\frac{|C_{i}|}{|C_{j}|}\geq\Omega(1)
. Thus for a small enough constant
c^{\prime}
we have

s_{\min}\cdot||\mu||^{2}_{2}\geq\frac{s_{\min}}{2\cdot|C|}\geq c^{\prime}\text{,}

(176)

Thus by (
176
) and by choice of
s^{(1-80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\geq\frac{10^{6}}{c^{\prime}}\cdot k\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)^{2}\geq\frac{10^{6}\cdot k\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)^{2}}{s_{\min}\cdot||\mu||^{2}_{2}}
we get

\frac{\zeta^{2}\cdot||\mu||^{2}_{2}}{8}\geq 400\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{640\cdot k\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{s\cdot s_{\min}}\right)

(177)

and

\frac{\zeta^{2}\cdot||\mu||^{2}_{2}}{8}\geq 400\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)\left(\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}\cdot\frac{2\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{3\cdot s}\sqrt{\frac{160\cdot k}{s_{\min}}}\right)

(178)

Therefore since
s\geq c\cdot\left(k\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)^{(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/(1-(80\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2}))}
for large enough constant
c
, and putting (
6.4.1
), (
177
) and (
178
) together we get

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\left|\widehat{\mu}-{\mu^{\prime}}\right|\right|_{2}>\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}\right]\leq(k+1)\cdot e^{-200\cdot\log\left(\frac{k}{\delta}\right)}\leq\left(\frac{\delta}{k}\right)^{100}

Thus with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}-\left(\frac{\delta}{k}\right)^{100}\geq 1-\delta
we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot\|\mu\|_{2}\text{.}

(179)

Step
2
:

Next we want to bound
\|\mu-\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C^{\prime}}f_{x}-\frac{1}{|C|}\sum_{x\in C}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C}f_{x}-\frac{1}{|C|}\sum_{x\in C}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}+\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{1-(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})}-1\right)\left|\left|\mu\right|\right|_{2}+\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}

Since
|C^{\prime}|\geq\left(1-(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})\right)|C|
by (
167
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})\cdot\|\mu\|_{2}+\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}

(180)

It thus remains to upper bound the second term. We now note that

\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}||f_{x}||_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}\|f_{x}\|_{\infty}

(181)

For any
y\geq 1
we define

T(y)=\left\{x\in V:||f_{x}||_{\infty}\geq 2\cdot y\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{10}{s_{\min}}}\right\}

Therefore, by Lemma
38
we have

|T(y)|\leq k\cdot\left(\frac{2\cdot y\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}}{2}\right)^{-\varphi^{2}/(20\cdot\epsilon)}\cdot s_{\min}=\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{-2}\cdot y^{-\varphi^{2}/(20\cdot\epsilon)}\cdot s_{\min}\text{.}

(182)

Using the bound on
|T(y)|
above, we now get

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}\|f_{x}\|_{\infty}

(183)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}\left(y\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{40}{s_{\min}}}\right)\cdot|T(y)|\cdot dy

By definition of
T(y)
and
C^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{160}{s_{\min}}}\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\int_{1}^{\infty}y\cdot|T(y)|\cdot dy

Since
k^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\leq 2
for small enough
\frac{\epsilon\cdot\log k}{\varphi^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{160}{s_{\min}}}\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\int_{1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{-2}\cdot y^{\left(1-\varphi^{2}/(20\cdot\epsilon)\right)}\cdot s_{\min}\cdot dy

By (
182
)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{160}{s_{\min}}}\cdot s_{\min}\cdot\left(\frac{s}{\delta}\right)^{(40\cdot\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\cdot\left(\frac{s\cdot k}{\delta}\right)^{-2}\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}/(20\cdot\epsilon)-2}

Since for any
c<0,\int_{1}^{\infty}y^{c}dy=\frac{-1}{c+1}

\displaystyle\leq k^{-2}\cdot s^{-1}\cdot\sqrt{s_{\min}}

For small enough
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}

(184)

Therefore we get

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}\|f_{x}\|_{\infty}

By (
181
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}\cdot k^{-2}\cdot s^{-1}\cdot\sqrt{s_{\min}}}{|C^{\prime}|}

By (
184
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\cdot k^{-1}\cdot s^{-1}}{\sqrt{|C|}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s_{\min}}}{\sqrt{|C|}}

By (
167
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k^{-1}\cdot s^{-1}}{\sqrt{|C|}}

Since
|C|\geq s_{\min}

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot k^{-1}\cdot s^{-1}\cdot||\mu||_{2}

By Lemma
7

||\mu||_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2\cdot\sqrt{|C|}}

Therefore by (
180
) we have

\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{2}\leq 2\cdot(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2})\|\mu\|_{2}+\left|\left|\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|}\sum_{x\in C\setminus C^{\prime}}f_{x}\right|\right|_{2}\leq 2\left(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2}+\cdot k^{-1}\cdot s^{-1}\right)||\mu||_{2}\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}

(185)

The last inequality holds since
s\geq 8\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)^{2}
, hence,
2\left(k^{-1}\cdot s^{-2}\cdot\delta^{2}+\cdot k^{-1}\cdot s^{-1}\right)\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}
.
Putting (
168
), (
179
) and (
185
) together with probability at least
1-\delta
we get

||\widehat{\mu}-\mu||_{2}\leq||\widehat{\mu}-\mu^{\prime}||_{2}+||\mu^{\prime}-\mu||_{2}\leq\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}+\frac{\zeta}{2}\cdot||\mu||_{2}\leq\zeta\cdot||\mu||_{2}

∎

To conclude our argument we show that if we sample enough nodes, we have a large number of samples in each cluster.

Lemma 40
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
,
\frac{\epsilon\log k}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a positive sufficiently small constant. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
S
be the multiset of
s\in\Omega(k\log k)
vertices each sampled independently at random from
V
. Then with probability at least
\frac{9}{10}
, for every
i\in[k]
,

|S\cap C_{i}|\geq\frac{0.9\cdot s}{k}\cdot\min_{p,q\in[k]}\frac{|C_{p}|}{|C_{q}|}\text{.}

Proof.

For
i\in[k]
, and
1\leq r\leq s
, let
X_{i}^{r}
be a random variable which is
1
if the
r
-th sampled vertex is in
C_{i}
, and
0
otherwise. Thus
\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{r}]=\frac{|C_{i}|}{n}
. Observe that
|S\cap C_{i}|
is a random variable defined as
\sum_{r=1}^{s}X_{i}^{r}
, where its expectation is given by

\mathbb{E}[|S\cap C_{i}|]=\sum_{r=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{r}]=s\cdot\frac{|C_{i}|}{n}\geq\frac{s\cdot s_{\min}}{k\cdot s_{\max}}\text{.}

Notice that random variables
X_{i}^{r}
are independent, Therefore, by Chernoff bound,

\Pr\left[|S\cap C_{i}|<\frac{9s}{10}\cdot\frac{|C_{i}|}{n}\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{200}\cdot\frac{s\cdot s_{\min}}{k\cdot s_{\max}}\right)\text{.}

By union bound and since
s=500\cdot k\cdot\log k\cdot\frac{s_{\max}}{s_{\min}}
we have

\Pr\left[\exists i\text{: }|S\cap C_{i}|<\frac{9s}{10}\cdot\frac{|C_{i}|}{n}\right]\leq k\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{1}{200}\cdot\frac{s\cdot s_{\min}}{k\cdot s_{\max}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{10}\text{.}

Therefore with probability at least
\frac{9}{10}
for all
i\in[k]
we have

\displaystyle|S\cap C_{i}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{9\cdot s}{10}\cdot\frac{|C_{i}|}{n}\geq\frac{0.9\cdot s}{k}\cdot\frac{s_{\min}}{s_{\max}}

∎

6.4.2
Approximate Centers are strongly orthogonal

The main result of this section is Lemma
41
that generalizes Lemma
12
to the approximate of cluster means.

Lemma 41
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
, and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
0<\zeta<\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}
. Let
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}
denote the cluster means of
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
denote an approximation of the cluster means such that for each
i\in[k]
,
||\mu_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2}
. Let
S\subset\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}
denote a subset of cluster means.
Let
\widehat{\Pi}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
denote the orthogonal projection matrix into the
span({S})^{\perp}
. Then the following holds:

1.

For all
\widehat{\mu}_{i}\in\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus S
we have
\left|\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||_{2}^{2}\text{.}

2.

For all
\widehat{\mu}_{i}\neq\widehat{\mu}_{j}\in\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus S
we have
|\langle\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|\leq\frac{50\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}|\cdot|C_{j}|}}\text{.}

To prove Lemma
41
we use Lemma
30
from Section
4
and we prove Lemma
42
.

Lemma 42
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
\varphi\in(0,1)
, and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
. Let
0<\zeta<\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}
. Let
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
denote an approximation of the cluster means such that for each
i\in[k]
,
||\mu_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2}
. Let
S=\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus\{\widehat{\mu}_{i}\}
. Let
\widehat{H}=[\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{i-1},\widehat{\mu}_{i+1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}]
denote a matrix such that its columns are the vectors in
S
. Let
\widehat{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)\times(k-1)}
denote a diagonal matrix such that for all
j<i
we have
\widehat{W}(j,j)=\sqrt{|C_{j}|}
and for all
j\geq i
we have
\widehat{W}(j,j)=\sqrt{|C_{j+1}|}
. Let
\widehat{Z}=\widehat{H}\widehat{W}
. Then we have

\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\leq\frac{10\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\text{.}

Proof.

Note that
\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}=(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T})-|C_{i}|\widehat{\mu}_{i}\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}
. Thus we have

\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}=\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}\right)\widehat{\mu}_{i}-|C_{i}|\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{4}\text{.}

(186)

By Lemma
9
for any vector
x
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

x^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|{\mu}_{j}{\mu}_{j}^{T}-I\right)x\leq\frac{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

(187)

Note that

\displaystyle||\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}-\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|{\mu}_{j}{\mu}_{j}^{T}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}-{\mu}_{j}{\mu}_{j}^{T}||_{2}

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\left(||\left({\mu}_{j}+(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j})\right)\left({\mu}_{j}+(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j})\right)^{T}-{\mu}_{j}{\mu}_{j}^{T}||_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\left(||\left(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}\right)\left(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}\right)^{T}||_{2}+||{\mu}_{j}\left(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}\right)^{T}||_{2}+||\left(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}\right){\mu}_{j}^{T}||_{2}\right)

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\cdot(\zeta^{2}+2\zeta)\cdot||\mu_{j}||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\text{Since }||\widehat{\mu}_{j}-\mu_{j}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{j}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\cdot 6\cdot\zeta\cdot\frac{1}{|C_{j}|}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma \ref{lem:dotmu} }||\mu_{j}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{2}{|C_{i}|}

\displaystyle\leq 6\cdot\zeta\cdot k

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2\varphi}

\displaystyle\text{Since }\zeta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}

Thus for any vector
x
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have

x^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}-\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|{\mu}_{j}{\mu}_{j}^{T}\right)x\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2\varphi}

(188)

Putting (
188
) and (
187
) for any vector any vector
x
with
||x||_{2}=1
we have that

x^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}-I\right)x\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Hence we can write

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}\right)\widehat{\mu}_{i}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}-I\right)\widehat{\mu}_{i}+\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\leq\left(1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

Therefore by (
186
) we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}|C_{j}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{T}\right)\widehat{\mu}_{i}-|C_{i}|\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{4}\leq\left(1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}-|C_{i}|\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

By Lemma
7
, and since
||\widehat{\mu_{i}}||\geq(1-\zeta)||\mu_{i}||_{2}
and
\zeta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}
we have that

|C_{i}|\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\geq\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)(1-\zeta)^{2}\geq 1-\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}

Thus we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\leq\left(1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}-|C_{i}|\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}-1+\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{10\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

∎

We now prove the main result of this section (Lemma
41
).
See
41

Proof.

Proof of item (
1
):
Since
\widehat{\Pi}
is a orthogonal projection matrix we have
||\widehat{\Pi}||_{2}=1
. Hence, we have

||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\leq||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\text{.}

Thus it’s left to prove
||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\geq\left(1-\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}
. Note that by Pythagoras
||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}=||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}-||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}
. We will prove
||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}
which implies

||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\geq\left(1-20\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\text{.}

Thus in order to complete the proof we need to show
||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}
. Let
S^{\prime}=\{\widehat{\mu}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus\{\widehat{\mu}_{i}\}
. Let
\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime}
denote the orthogonal projection matrix into
span(S^{\prime})^{\perp}
. Note that
S\subseteq S^{\prime}
, hence
span(S)
is a subspace of
span(S^{\prime})
, therefore we have
||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq||(I-\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}
. Thus it suffices to prove
||(I-\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}
. Let
\widehat{H}=[\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{i-1},\widehat{\mu}_{i+1},\ldots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}]
denote a matrix such that its columns are the vectors in
S^{\prime}
. Let
\widehat{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k-1)\times(k-1)}
denote a diagonal matrix such that for all
j<i
we have
\widehat{W}(j,j)=\sqrt{|C_{j}|}
and for all
j\geq i
we have
\widehat{W}(j,j)=\sqrt{|C_{j+1}|}
. Let
\widehat{Z}=\widehat{H}\widehat{W}
. Then the orthogonal projection matrix onto the span of
S^{\prime}
is defined as
(I-\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime})=\widehat{Z}(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}\widehat{Z}^{T}
. By Lemma
30
item (
2
),
(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}
is spectrally close to
I
, hence,
(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}
exists. Therefore we have

\displaystyle||(I-\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}(\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}((\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}-I)\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}+\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}

(189)

By Lemma
30
item (
2
) we have

\left|\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\left((\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}-I\right)\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

(190)

Thus we get

\displaystyle||(I-\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}((\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{Z})^{-1}-I)\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}+\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}

By (
189
)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{5\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}+1\right)||\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

By (
190
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot||\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\text{For small enough }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}

By Lemma
42
we have

||\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}=\widehat{\mu}_{i}^{T}\widehat{Z}\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\leq\frac{10\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

Therefore we get

||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq||(I-\widehat{\Pi}^{\prime})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq 2||\widehat{Z}^{T}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}

(191)

Hence,

||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}_{2}\geq\left(1-20\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}^{2}\right)\text{.}

Proof of item (
2
):

Note that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}+\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i},(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{j}+\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle=\langle(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i},(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle+\langle\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle

Thus by triangle inequality we have

|\langle\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|\leq|\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|+|\langle(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i},(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|

By Cauchy-Schwarz we have

\displaystyle|\langle(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i},(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}||(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||_{2}||\widehat{\mu}_{j}||_{2}

By (
191
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{40\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma \ref{lem:dotmu} and }||\widehat{\mu_{i}}-\mu_{i}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2}

Also for any
i,j\in[k]
we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mu_{i},\mu_{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle{\mu}_{i}+(\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i}),{\mu}_{j}+(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j})\right\rangle-\left\langle{\mu}_{i},{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle|+|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i},\mu_{j}\right\rangle|+|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j},\mu_{i}\right\rangle|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\ ||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i}||_{2}||\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}||_{2}+||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-{\mu}_{i}||_{2}||\mu_{j}||_{2}+||\widehat{\mu}_{j}-{\mu}_{j}||_{2}||\mu_{i}||_{2}

By Cauchy-Schwarz

\displaystyle\leq(\zeta^{2}+2\zeta)\left(||\mu_{i}||_{2}||\mu_{j}||_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\text{Since }||\widehat{\mu_{i}}-\mu_{i}||_{2}\leq\zeta||\mu_{i}||_{2}\text{ for all }i

\displaystyle\leq 6\cdot\zeta\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma \ref{lem:dotmu} }||\mu_{i}||^{2}_{2}\leq\frac{2}{|C_{i}|}\text{ for all }i

(192)

Note that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|\left\langle{\mu}_{i},{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle|+|\left\langle{\mu}_{i},{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\right\rangle|

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}+6\zeta\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}

By Lemma
7
and (
192
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}

\displaystyle\text{Since }\zeta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{20\cdot k\cdot\varphi}

Therefore we get

|\langle\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|\leq|\langle\widehat{\mu}_{i},\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|+|\langle(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{i},(I-\widehat{\Pi})\widehat{\mu}_{j}\rangle|\leq\frac{50\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{|C_{i}||C_{j}|}}\text{.}

∎

6.5
Partitioning scheme works with
approximate
cluster means & dot products

In Section
6.3
we showed that the partitioning scheme works if we have access to real centers (i.e.
\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}
), to exact dot product evaluations (i.e
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
) and
OuterConductance
is precise.

In this section we show that approximations to all above is enough for the partitioning scheme to work. More precisely we show that if we have access only to
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}\approx\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
, the search procedure finds
\hat{\mu}_{i}
’s that are only approximately equal to
\mu_{i}
’s and
OuterConductance
is only approximately correct then
FindCenters
still succeeds with high probability.

In order to prove such a statement we first show a technical Lemma (
Lemma
43
), that relates the approximate dot product with approximate centers to the dot product with the actual cluster centers.

Note that the following Lemma
43
works for any
S\subset\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}
and the corresponding
\widehat{S}
. This is useful for application in Lemma
45
because it allows to reason about candidate sets
\widehat{C}^{(}T_{1},\dots,T_{b})_{\widehat{\mu}}
, after we associate
\bigcup_{i\in[b]}T_{i}
with
\widehat{S}
.

Lemma 43
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\varphi\in(0,1)
,
\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}
be smaller than a sufficiently small constant.
Let
G=(V,E)
be a
d
-regular graph that admits a
(k,\varphi,\epsilon)
-clustering
C_{1},\dots,C_{k}
.
Then conditioned on the success of the spectral dot product oracle the following conditions hold.

Let
\widehat{\mu}_{1},\widehat{\mu}_{2},\dots,\widehat{\mu}_{k}
be such that for all
i\in[k]

\|\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{i}\|^{2}\leq 10^{-12}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}\cdot k^{2}}\|\mu_{i}\|^{2}
. Let
i\in[k]
and
S\subseteq\{\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{k}\}\setminus\{\mu_{i}\}
and
\widehat{S}\subseteq\{\hat{\mu}_{1},\dots,\hat{\mu}_{k}\}\setminus\{\hat{\mu}_{i}\}
be the corresponding subset to
S
. Let
\Pi
be the orthogonal projection onto
span(S)^{\perp}
and
\widehat{\Pi}
be the orthogonal projection onto
span(\widehat{S})^{\perp}
.
Let also
\pi_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{k}\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{R}^{k}
be the projection onto the subspace spanned by
\Pi\mu_{i}
and
\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}
. Then if
\|\Pi_{i}f_{x}\|^{2}\leq\frac{10^{4}}{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}
then:

\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\rangle}{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|\leq 0.02

Furthermore if
\widehat{\mu}_{i}
’s are averages of
s
points, then
\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}
can be computed in
\widetilde{O}_{\varphi}\left(s^{4}\cdot\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1/2+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)
time with preprocessing time of
\widetilde{O}_{\varphi}\left(\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1/2+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)
and space
\widetilde{O}_{\varphi}\left(\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1/2+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)

Proof.

First we prove the runtime guarantee and then we show correctness.

Runtime.

We first bound the running time.
If we set the precision parameter of Algorithm
6
to
\xi=10^{-6}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}
then by Theorem
2
the preprocessing time takes

\widetilde{O}_{\varphi}\left(\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1/2+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)
time,
\widetilde{O}_{\varphi}\left(\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1/2+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)
space, and by Corollary
1

computing
\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}
takes

\widetilde{O}_{\varphi}\left(s^{4}\cdot\left(\frac{k}{\epsilon}\right)^{O(1)}\cdot n^{1/2+O(\epsilon/\varphi^{2})}\right)
time.

Correctness.

Now we show that we also obtain a good approximation. We will show it in two steps:

1.

\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\rangle}{\|\Pi\mu_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\leq 0.01

2.

\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\rangle}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|\leq 0.01

If we are able to prove
1
and
2
then the claim of the Lemma follows from triangle inequality.

Before we present the two proofs we show a useful fact:

\displaystyle||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\Pi\mu_{i}||

\displaystyle\leq||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{i}||+||\Pi\mu_{i}-\mu_{i}||+||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{i}||

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20\epsilon^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\varphi}}||\hat{\mu}_{i}||+\frac{16\epsilon^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\varphi}}||\mu_{i}||+10^{-6}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi\cdot k}\|\mu_{i}\|

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma~\ref{lem:apxdosubspace},~\ref{lem:dosubspace} and the bound on }\|\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{40\epsilon^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\varphi}}||\mu_{i}||

\displaystyle\text{As }\|\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{i}\|^{2}\leq 10^{-12}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}\cdot k^{2}}\|\mu_{i}\|^{2}

(193)

Proof of
1
:
Notice that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\rangle}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\rangle}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle f_{x},\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\Pi_{i}f_{x},\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\text{By definition of }\pi_{i}

\displaystyle\leq||\Pi_{i}f_{x}||\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

By Cauchy-Schwarz

(194)

First we will upper bound
\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|
. We split it into two cases:

Case 1.

If
\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}\geq\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}
then we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{(1-\frac{16\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi})||\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

By Lemma
12

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{(1-\frac{16\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi})||\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{(1+\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi})(1+10^{-12}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}\cdot k^{2}})||\mu_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

Lemma
41
, assumptions

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{||\mu_{i}||^{2}}\left|\left|\Pi\mu_{i}-\left(1-\frac{1600\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right|\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{||\mu_{i}||^{2}}\left(\left|\left|\frac{1600\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\Pi\mu_{i}\right|\right|+\left(1-\frac{1600\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\Pi\mu_{i}||\right)

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{12800\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\frac{1}{||\mu_{i}||}

By (
193
) and Lemma
12

Case 2.

If
\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}<\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}
then we have:

\displaystyle\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{(1+\frac{16\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi})||\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

By Lemma
12

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{(1+\frac{16\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi})||\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{(1-\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi})(1-10^{-12}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}\cdot k^{2}})||\mu_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|

Lemma
41
, assumptions

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{||\mu_{i}||^{2}}\left|\left|\Pi\mu_{i}-\left(1+\frac{1600\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right|\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{||\mu_{i}||^{2}}\left(\left|\left|\frac{1600\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\Pi\mu_{i}\right|\right|+\left(1+\frac{1600\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\Pi\mu_{i}||\right)

By triangle inequality

\displaystyle\leq\frac{12800\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\frac{1}{||\mu_{i}||}

By (
193
) and Lemma
12

Combining the two cases we get:

\left|\left|\frac{\Pi\mu_{i}}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|\right|\leq\frac{12800\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\frac{1}{||\mu_{i}||}\text{.}

Substituting into (
194
) we get:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\Pi\mu_{i}\rangle}{||\Pi\mu_{i}||^{2}}-\frac{\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\rangle}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq||\Pi_{i}f_{x}||\cdot\frac{12800\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\frac{1}{||\mu_{i}||}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{100}{\sqrt{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}}\cdot\frac{12800\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\frac{1}{||\mu_{i}||}

By assumption of the Lemma

\displaystyle\leq 0.005\frac{1}{\sqrt{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}\cdot||\mu_{i}||}

\displaystyle\text{As }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ is sufficiently small and }\frac{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}=O(1)

\displaystyle\leq 0.01

By Lemma
7

Proof of
2
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}-10^{-6}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot n^{-1}

By Corollary
1
, setting of
\xi
and assumptions

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{20\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot||\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}-0.01\cdot n^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma~\ref{lem:apxdosubspace} and }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-10^{-12}\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}\cdot k}\right)\cdot 0.99\cdot||\mu_{i}||^{2}-0.01\cdot n^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{By }||\widehat{\mu}_{i}-\mu_{i}||^{2}\leq 10^{-12}\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}\cdot k}||\mu_{i}||^{2}\text{ and }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\right)\cdot 0.98\cdot n^{-1}-0.01\cdot n^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma~\ref{lem:dotmu}, }|C_{i}|\leq n\text{, }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

\displaystyle\geq 0.5\cdot n^{-1}

\displaystyle\text{As }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

(195)

Next notice that:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\rangle}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\rangle}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|+\left|\frac{10^{-6}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot n^{-1}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|

By Corollary
1

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle\left(\frac{1}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right)\right|+\left|\frac{10^{-6}\cdot n^{-1}}{0.5\cdot n^{-1}}\right|

\displaystyle\text{By \eqref{eq:mihatapxnormlwrbnd} and }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle\right|\left|\frac{1}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|+10^{-5}

(196)

Now we will separately bound
\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle\right|
and
\left|\frac{1}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|
from (
196
). As
|\left\langle a,b\right\rangle|\leq||a||\cdot||b||
we get:

\left|\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle\right|\leq||\Pi_{i}f_{x}||\cdot||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||

(197)

Now we bound the second term from (
196
):

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}-||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{10^{-6}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot n^{-1}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|

Corollary
1
, setting of
\xi
and assumptions

\displaystyle\leq 10^{-5}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\left|\frac{0.5\cdot n^{-1}}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||^{2}\cdot 0.5\cdot n^{-1}}\right|

By (
195
)

\displaystyle\leq 10^{-5}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\left|\frac{1}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||\cdot(||\Pi\mu_{i}||-\frac{40\epsilon^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\varphi}}||\mu_{i}||)}\right|

By (
193
)

\displaystyle\leq 10^{-4}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{1}{||\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}||\cdot||\mu_{i}||}

\displaystyle\text{By Lemma~\ref{lem:dosubspace} and }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small}

(198)

Substituting (
197
) and (
198
) in (
196
) we get:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\rangle}{\|\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|^{2}}-\frac{\left\langle f_{x},\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rangle_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}}{\left\lVert\widehat{\Pi}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\right\rVert_{\scriptscriptstyle apx}^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 10^{-5}+10^{-4}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{||\Pi_{i}f_{x}||}{||\mu_{i}||}

\displaystyle\leq 10^{-5}+10^{-4}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{\varphi}\cdot\frac{100}{\sqrt{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}}\cdot\frac{1}{||\mu_{i}||}

By assumption

\displaystyle\leq 10^{-5}+10^{-3}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}\cdot||\mu_{i}||}

\displaystyle\text{As }\frac{\epsilon}{\varphi^{2}}\text{ small, }\frac{\max_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}{\min_{p\in[k]}|C_{p}|}=O(1)

\displaystyle\leq 0.01

By Lemma
7

∎

Now we are ready to show that there exist an algorithm (Algorithm
11
) that can estimate accurately the size of candidate clusters of the form
\widehat{C}^{(T_{1},\dots,T_{b})}_{\widehat{\mu}}
